- **Every teleport carries the implement too** (v1.131, owner: *"we need to
  anchor the tractor when it tps because the tool drags it, also the tractor tps
  before the tool can get back in place"*). The v1.129 wait made the swing
  visible; it could not make the hitch keep up, because `Model:PivotTo` moves
  **only that model** — the implement is a separate model joined to the ride by
  the game's own constraint, so each hop left the tool on the old ground and put
  the constraint under the whole length of the trip. The live v1.130 console
  measured it: a 286-stud arc hop → **`hitch gap at arrival: 277.7 studs`**, then
  `the S4204 is 210 studs from the ride — it is not on the hitch any more` on
  every pass and `fired 0 of 2398 cell(s) … 0 took`. A taut tow is both reported
  symptoms: it drags the ride, and the settle wait (≤1.5 s) can never close it,
  so the next jump fires while the tool is still catching up.
  `spudPivotRig(ride, cf, tool)` (a **global**, so it costs no build-closure
  register) pivots the ride and then translates the implement by the **same
  position delta**, keeping the tool's own rotation — a ride turn must not spin
  the plow. **A self-propelled job's `tool` IS the ride** (the harvest tour sets
  `impl = ride`), so the `tool ~= ride` guard is not decoration: without it the
  delta is applied twice and the harvester lands **twice the trip away** — both
  harnesses pin that case. Every teleport goes through it: the **grid** stops and their
  re-parks (`spudRadialFire`), the **per-cell hop** and its re-park
  (`spudHopCells`), and every pivot of the **arc hop** (`hopTo`, which gained the
  `towed` parameter; the field tour passes the hitched implement — or the ride
  itself, which the guard turns into a plain hop — and the unload trips pass
  `nil`). The gap is then only the tool's own
  trailing distance, so the settle wait reads `settled`, the `far` bail stays as
  the backstop for a tool that comes off the hitch anyway, and the whole field
  gets called at instead of refused. `1000016.py`'s fixture is now
  honest about the game — a ride `PivotTo` **does not** move the fake implement —
  and asserts the carry in both passes (a 15-cell field makes 15 jumps **and**
  15 carries) plus ten tie checks that no bare `ride:PivotTo` survives in them;
  `1000024.py` pins the arc (24 carries over 25 moves, the hitch still exactly
  20 studs, and a control that a ride-only hop opens a 286-stud gap).
- **The tow is carried, NEVER welded** (v1.133 welded it, **v1.134 deleted the
  weld** — owner: *"bro make it anchor te tool permanantly behind the tractor
  while it's hitched"*, then, from the same build live: *"it's making the wheels
  glitch"*). The idea was that a carried tool still hangs on the game's own
  constraint, so what arrives behind the ride is whatever the solver managed in
  the frames available and every grid stop has to re-aim from a fresh reading; a
  client **`WeldConstraint`** between the two hitch parts (`SpudTowAnchor`,
  `Part0` = the ride's, `Part1` = the tool's, the pair `findHitchPart` ranks)
  after `SetNetworkOwner(LocalPlayer)` on the implement would make ride +
  implement one assembly and end the swing. **It was measured, and the measurement
  refutes it.** The emulator console answered open question (a) of v1.133 — the
  live server *does* accept a client-created weld — and that is the defect:
  `anchor: JDShank welded to the ChallengerMT9's HitchRoot`, `hitch gap at
  arrival: 1.1 studs` (the swing really did go away), **and then a wheel lying on
  the field beside a tractor reading `Speed 0`** in the screenshot, while the very
  next grid logged `remote plow: fired 9 of 98 cell(s) from 12 stop(s), 3x3 per
  stop — 0 took, 98 left` (the same field without the weld took 1793 of 2388).
  Root cause, from Roblox's own assembly rules rather than a guess: **the game's
  hitch constraint already joins those two models into one assembly**, so a second
  joint between the same pair is a loop, and a loop is over-constrained — the
  solver throws a part off. And the ownership claim moved the merged assembly to
  this client while the server keeps simulating the implement, so client and
  server disagree about where the blade is, which is exactly what a field call is
  judged on. So `spudAnchorTool` / `spudUnanchorTool` / `spudAnchor` are **deleted**
  with all eight of their call sites (the park, the unhitch path, the blind X at
  attempt 3, the wrong-tool grab, the bottom of `hitchTool`, loop end, loop stop,
  `destroyUI`), and the only `SetNetworkOwner` left is the pre-existing
  `claimOwnership` on the ride the player drives. The swing stays handled the way
  that is proven to work: v1.131's carry plus v1.132's per-stop re-aim.
  `tools/1000040.py` replaces the weld harness and refuses the build if
  any weld symbol comes back, and the note in the source says
  *"Do not re-add a weld to the tow."*
- **The line-up has two measurements, not one** (v1.135, live 2026-11-03): the
  physical park read a `BasePart` off each machine through `findHitchPart`, and
  on the Tempo build the ride answered **`tractor=none`** in every run while the
  implement answered `plow=Hitch` — so `lineUpHitch` had nothing to measure, said
  `no hitch parts to measure from (tractor=none, plow=Hitch) — creeping` and spent
  all ten attempts creeping. The second source is the game's **own socket pair**,
  the exact instances the captured `FNCAttemptHitch` takes: `Components.Hitchable`
  on the ride and `Chassis.HitchableSocket` on the tool. **They are not both
  attachments** (v1.153, live 2026-11-04): the Tempo planter answers
  `Chassis.HitchableSocket` as an `Attachment`, while the HorschJoker answers the
  same name as a **Folder** wrapping the real point —
  `HitchableSocket/SocketRoot [Part]` → `SocketRoot/SocketOrigin [Attachment]`.
  A line-up that only reads the first shape calls a socketed tool socket-less, so
  the creep ate all ten attempts and the cultivator ran the whole pass unhitched
  (`hitch: gave up on HorschJoker after 10 attempt(s) — this pass runs without
  it`). Global `spudSocketPoint(socket)` now reads every shape the game has shown:
  the instance itself when it is an Attachment (its `WorldCFrame`) or a Part (its
  `CFrame`), else the **shallowest** join inside it — an attachment first, a part
  if there is no attachment, the walk guarded and capped at 64 descendants, and
  **nil** when there is nothing to read, which stays the miss line's business
  (rule 7: no point invented for a socket that cannot be found). `hitchPoint`
  asks for a part first, then hands the named socket to it, and the log names
  which instance the point came from (`HitchableSocket.SocketOrigin (the game's
  socket)`). A read that still fails is
  **named, not guessed past** (rule 7): `spudHitchMissText` prints the model's
  name, how many instances the client actually holds, and whether it is
  `IsDescendantOf(workspace)` at all — `tractor TempoL16 … has no hitch point
  (N instance(s) read, and it is NOT in the world)` — instead of a bare `none`.
  **Since v1.146 that miss also names what it did read**: up to eight instances
  on the model whose own name says `hitch` / `socket` / `attach` / `connector`,
  each with class and path, or the honest `nothing on it is named like a hitch,
  socket or attachment` — because live the HorschJoker answers `(309 instance(s)
  read)` on every run and a count does not tell this build what the game calls
  its join (rule 4). The tow is never *forced*: the client-side weld that would
  do it was measured in v1.133 and deleted in v1.134, because the game's own
  hitch constraint already puts both models in one assembly.
  The line-up's pivot carries the tow (`spudPivotRig(tr, newPivot, airborne and
  nil or plow)`); the pre-hitch arc deliberately flies the ride alone, because a
  welded-free carry through a 288-stud arc is what opens the gap. The same two
  points are re-read at **arrival** (`hitch gap at arrival: N studs`), so the gap
  the owner sees in the console is measured by the same rule that aimed the park.
- **A seed round that takes nothing cannot run for ever** (v1.135, live): the
  same build looped `seeding: 2362 cell(s) still need seed — teleport round 12`
  because a round only ended when the count reached zero. `seedPasses` now keeps
  `leftWas`/`leftStuck`: **two rounds in a row that leave the count unchanged**
  end the pass with `N cell(s) still need seed after 2 round(s) that took nothing
  — leaving them for the next pass (the hitch and tool lines above say why)`, and
  a refill that puts seeds in resets the count. The "keep going while it makes
  progress" rule is unchanged.
- **Teleports only** (v1.95, owner 2026-10-31: *"during the plowing it
  started doing the fast tp and then kinda strafing the ground"*). Between
  the grid and the hop there used to be a **serpentine tween sweep**
  (`spudSweepCells`, 45 studs/s with a burst every 0.5 s) — the one thing
  that slid the ride along the ground. It is **deleted** with its harness
  checks: a field pass is the call pass → the 3×3 block grid →
  `spudHopCells` (one teleport per cell) → grid → hop, round after round,
  and a cell neither takes is named and left for the next pass. Nothing
  in a field pass touches a movement key or a tween.
- **It runs from the field, hitched and seated** — the same state the game fires
  the call from: the attempt sits at the top of `sweepField`, after the ride has
  arrived and the tool is hitched.
- **Plow / seed / harvest are siblings.** Only cultivate was recorded; the other
  three use the same `(field, {id}, {attachment})` shape at the same path
  (`EVTPlowFieldCell` FireServer, `FNCSeedFieldCell` / `FNCHarvestFieldCell`
  InvokeServer). If a build disagrees, the verification disables just that job
  instead of corrupting the pass.
- **Seeder fill** — `spudFillSeederRemote(seeder)` calls the captured
  `FNCFillSeeder(seeder)`; `buySeedAndFill` still presses V when the seed count
  does not rise, so a refusal costs one extra press, not a failed trip.
- **Fill to the machine's OWN capacity** (v1.133, owner: *"if a seeder has like
  600 capasity, make it fill up the seeder fully"* → *"fill to max, trips scales
  to fix. by the way you can carry to seed crates at once"*). A seeder is no
  longer topped up with one crate: the pour loop runs while
  `spudSeederWantsMore(count, cap)` (i.e. `count < cap`, and **false** when either
  number is unreadable — an unknown pair is never "hungry") and the hands still
  hold a crate, so a 600-slot machine gets 600 seeds instead of the first box's
  50. Three globals carry the arithmetic (all **pure**, so the harness can slice
  them; the loop they drive is nested inside `buySeedAndFill` and is pinned by
  source ties instead):
  - `spudCratesPerTrip = 2` — the game's own carry limit, so one town visit buys
    **two** crates. After the first purchase is proven the run prices and carries
    a second crate, proven by the `CarryLinks` **child count**
    (`spudCratesInHand()`) rather than by `carrying()`, which is already true with
    one box in hand. A second crate dearer than the *post-first-buy* balance is
    left on the shelf, and anything bought-but-unpaid is put back down
    (`spudDropRemote` until the hands read empty) before the fill, so a shelf box
    can never reach the seeder.
  - `spudSeedTripsNeeded(cap, have, perCrate, cratesPerTrip)` =
    `ceil(ceil(room / perCrate) / cratesPerTrip)`, and **nil** when the capacity or
    the crate size is unreadable (rule 7 — that is unknown, not one trip).
    `spudSeedsPerCrate` is **learned** from a real pour (the count a rise actually
    gave), never guessed at 50, so until a crate has been poured the ceiling stays
    at the old constant.
  - `spudSeedTripCeiling(needed, floorMax)` only ever **raises** the floor — 4 for
    the entry check, 3 for a mid-field refill — so a big tank gets the trips it
    needs (`refill 1/6`, not the unreachable `refill 1/3`) and a 50-slot seeder
    behaves exactly as before. Both call sites read it; the price gate still sits
    **before** the trip is counted.
  - **The cap the plan uses is the cap this build has actually READ** (v1.143,
    owner: *"when it puts seeds back in the seeder it isnt filling it up to 600
    only about 100"*). That floor is exactly the "only about 100": the machine is
    one model, but the two gates ask it separately, at different moments, and a
    part that is still streaming answers **nil**. Live, in one console:
    `✅ seeder now holds 50/600 seeds (Rice)` → `refill 1/6: buying Rice and
    filling` → `this run already made 4 shop trip(s) (the cap is 4)`. **6** and
    **4** are the same seeder read twice — 600 once, nil once — and a nil plan
    collapses to the floor, so a 600-slot tank got roughly one crate a visit and
    was then called loaded. `seederSeedState` now remembers a cap it read
    (`if cap and cap > 0 then spudSeederCap = cap end` — guarded, because an
    unconditional write would let a failed read wipe the 600), and
    `spudSeederCapFor(cap, remembered)` is the only way a plan gets a number:
    a fresh read wins, a failed read falls back and says so (`via ==
    "remembered"`, printed as `(the cap is %d, planned on a %s tank of %s)`), and
    neither-read is still **nil** — unknown, not 600 dressed up as a number
    (rule 7). Both budget sites go through it. The pour also stops lying: when
    the hands are empty and `spudSeederWantsMore` is still true the line names
    the seeds left **and** does the town-visit arithmetic
    (`the hands are empty and the tank still wants N seed(s) — M more town
    visit(s) at 2 crate(s) a trip`), instead of `✅ seeder now holds 50/600`
    reading like a loaded machine.
  - **…and the visits are counted in crates the trip ACTUALLY bought** (v1.144,
    the other half of the same report). The owner's console proved the ask and
    the delivery are not the same number: `seed crate: the second purchase did
    not land — putting the crates back down and picking up only the one that is
    paid for`, then `✅ seeder now holds 50/600 seeds (Potato)`. A 600-slot tank
    at 50 a crate is **twelve** crates, and a plan built on the two-crate ask
    allows six visits — 300 seeds, and the tank is still called empty. Each
    finished trip now records `spudCratesTripGot = 1 + extraCrates`, every
    budget divides by the global `spudCratesTripActual()` (the learned count,
    never above the ask, the constant until a trip has finished), and the
    refusal line prints the whole plan: `the cap is 12, planned on a read tank
    of 600 at 1 crate(s) a trip`. `spudCratesPerTrip` stays the **request** the
    second-crate loop makes — nothing plans on it any more.
  - **…and one FILL is not one crate** (v1.145, the last third of the same
    report). The owner's next console proved the till does sometimes sell both
    boxes — `✅ second Potato crate bought via FNCRequestStorePurchase (balance
    1375937 → 1255937) — the seeder gets both from this one trip` — and the
    seeder still stopped at 101:
    `seeder fill: FNCFillSeeder(seeder) — the seeder had 1 seeds`, then **no
    pour round at all**, then `the hands are empty and the tank still wants 499
    seed(s) of 600 — 3 more town visit(s) at 2 crate(s) a trip`. **`FNCFillSeeder`
    drains every crate in hand in one call**, so the rise the build learned as
    "the size of one crate" was 100 — two crates — and the arithmetic then
    planned 3 visits (`3 × 2 × 100`) for a shortfall that needs 10 crates. The
    pour now reads `spudCratesInHand()` on both sides of the call and divides
    the rise by the crates it actually consumed (floored, still only ever
    raised, so a half-poured box cannot teach a size smaller than a full one),
    and the round line says both numbers:
    `round N poured 100 more from 2 crate(s) (50 a crate)`. The short-pour line
    prints the size it planned on too.
  - **…and the CRATE IS ASKED, not inferred** (v1.153, the same report a fifth
    time). That divider turned out to be as unreliable as the press: live, a
    two-crate trip raised the seeder `402/600 → 502/600 → 600/600` — 100 seeds a
    visit — and the console still said
    `the tank still wants 198 seed(s) of 600 — 1 more town visit(s) at 2 crate(s)
    a trip, 99 seed(s) a crate`. So `spudCratesInHand()` answered **one** box (or
    zero, and the `or 1` fallback fired), and the rise was credited to a single
    crate again. The consequence is not a cosmetic number: with 99 a crate,
    803 cells plan at `spudSeedTripsNeeded(803, 0, 99, 2)` = **5** visits, so the
    run read `the seeder still wants seed and this run already made 6 shop
    trip(s) (the cap is 5, planned on a remembered tank of 600 for 803 cell(s))`
    — a ceiling BELOW the trips already spent, which can only refuse — and ended
    `run ended — the last stage it reached was: shop trip: the seeder is empty`
    with 803 cells cultivated and never planted. **The crate's own
    `Configurations.Capacity` is the game's number for exactly this** (v1.116
    already reads it to skip an empty box), so global
    `spudCrateSize(own, learned)` takes it and the pour site asks the crate before
    it does any arithmetic: a readable crate is written **straight through** — it
    may correct a bad lesson in either direction — and the rise-divided-by-crates
    path survives only as the fallback for a box that cannot be read, still only
    ever raising. 803 cells then plan at 50 a crate = **9** visits, which is the
    truth (`17 crates at 2 a trip`).
  - **…and the tank is filled BEFORE the field, until the money says stop**
    (v1.148, owner: *"it should fill untill max or there is not mroe moeny"*,
    asked a third time as *"it's not filling the seeder all the way full before
    starting to seed"*). Two ways the build started planting on a partly-full
    machine. **(a)** `seedCheck` returned "ready" on **any** seed —
    `if count > 0 then … going to hitch it; return true` — so a seeder the last
    pass left at 48/600 went to the field and planted 48 cells. In the planting
    stage that branch now asks the machine: `hungry = allowBuy and cap and cap >
    0 and spudSeederWantsMore(count, cap)`, and a hungry tank falls through to
    the fill instead of returning true. **(b)** an empty tank made **one** trip
    and went — live: `✅ the seeder holds 50 seed(s) now — seeding this run`
    then `remote seed: fired 48 of 1898 cell(s) … 48 took`. `seedCheck` now
    loops the proven trip while the tank still reads room, counting each
    iteration against the same scaled ceiling, and the owner's "or there is not
    more money" is already the trip's price gate: an unaffordable crate returns
    false without spending, so the loop ends on the wallet rather than hammering
    it. The end line says which stop it hit — `the tank is full, seeding this
    run`, or `the money or the trip budget stopped the fill — planting with what
    it holds`. **A balance the log prints is the wallet, not a frame of the
    animation** (same build): the buy line reported `129026 → 87069` for a
    120,000 crate whose real remainder was **9,026** — the proof-of-purchase
    breaks on the first drop it sees and the HUD counter ticks down — so the
    report now takes one settled re-read while the drop stays the proof.
  - **…and the trip budget is sized by the FIELD, not by one tank** (v1.150,
    the same report a fourth time: *"STILL not filling the planter all the way
    nor planting the whole field"*). The buffer proved the fill works
    (`trip 6 of 6` → `✅ the seeder holds 600/600 seed(s) now — the tank is
    full`) and the field still finished at `1800 cultivated, 600 seeded, 2400
    total`. `spudSeedTripsNeeded(600, 0, 50, 2)` is **6** — six visits fills the
    *machine* — and both ceilings were planned on that, so a 2400-cell field
    spent its whole budget on the first 600 cells and every later refill was
    refused by the same number (`the cap is 6, planned on a remembered tank of
    600` → `the refill put no seeds in the seeder — stopping this pass`). The
    entry plan now takes the larger of filling the tank and covering
    `needCells`, and each refill round re-sizes `refillsMax` from the count it
    just re-read; 2400 cells is 48 crates, **24** visits at two crates each.
    The refusal names the field too (`… tank of 600 for 2400 cell(s) at 2
    crate(s) a trip`), an unreadable field stays **nil** (rule 7), and the
    price gate is still what stops a broke run.
- **Buying seeds from anywhere is NOT a thing** (owner: *"in earlier explot check
  diddnt you say that we could buy seedcs from anywhere? if no then drop it"*).
  It was never measured — the only answered shop questions are **Fill from afar**
  (refused at 963 studs) and **Spawn from afar** (works from anywhere), so no
  remote here is assumed to ignore proximity and nothing is wired for it.
- **Tests tab · batch field remote** (v1.43, user: *"can we just abuse bugs to
  plow and harvest"*). The captured call already carries a **list** of ids
  (`{1=641}`), so with the Tests toggle on (`spudBatchRemotes`) the pass first
  fires **one call with every remaining id** via `spudFieldRemoteCallMany` and
  re-reads the stages: all gone = `✅ batch test: N cell(s) in ONE call` and the
  sweep is skipped; some taken = the rest go one call per cell; none taken =
  `batch test: the server ignored the id list … — one call per cell` and the
  normal per-cell pass runs. It is **on by default** since v1.88 (owner: *"can
  you turn batch remotes on by default"*), session-only, and an
  ignored list never switches a job off. The batch's own takes are counted into
  the final `✅ remote …` line.
- **Tests tab · live probes** (trimmed in v1.60 and again in v1.74, user:
  *"remove any tool we arenet using anymore in the bugs tab becuase it's
  getting crowsed"*). The tab now holds the **Batch field remote** toggle and
  one probe (`spudTestRun`, `spudTestWork`, `spudTestSay`; lines land in the
  Tests paragraph and as `test:` lines, and `spudTestCopy` puts place id +
  build + player + the whole `spudTestLog` on the clipboard with
  `setclipboard`):
  - **Remote list** (v1.59, user: *"we need to find another way of forcing the
    seeding and plowing"*) — read-only. Walks `ReplicatedStorage` (Modules
    first, depth 4, 3000 nodes — v1.124: the cap was 600, so the v1.123 report's
    "339 found (601 nodes walked)" was a **truncated** list that did not say so;
    it now names `CAP 3000 REACHED` when it runs out). The per-cell field calls
    are only the ones the game fired by hand; a bulk/field door that was never
    fired would show up here and nowhere else.
  - **Field remotes** (v1.66) — the three field calls the capture never fired.
    `FNCSprayFieldCell(field, {id}, {att})` is tried with the plow/seed shape and
    judged on `IsWatered` / `PlantGrowth` / `Stage`; `FNCEditField()` and
    `FNCConvertRundownField()` are invoked with **no arguments** purely to read
    the shape they demand — an error names it, and nothing that could rebuild
    the field is handed to them. Live (2026-10-31, twice): the spray call runs
    and the cell does **not** change (Empty and Plowed both), `FNCEditField()`
    returns a **table** (a reader, not a setter — still just an address, which is
    what v1.124's config read inside **Grow now** exists to dump) and
    `FNCConvertRundownField()` refuses with
    `ConstructionHandler:686: attempt to index nil with 'Name'` — it wants an
    instance, so it is not a free field reset. **The spray leg was lying (v1.124).**
    The v1.123 run reached a real crop (`cell 518 is Seeded, watered false,
    growth 1170 (tool none)`) and still said `no change from here`, because
    `spudToolAttachment` had found no implement, the third slot got a bare `nil`
    instead of the captured `{att}` **list**, and an empty request was reported as
    a verdict. It now sends `{ att }`, names the attachment it sent (or says no
    implement exists and that a `no change` **answers nothing**), and points at
    **Grow now** for the sprayer-by-name test. So **water on a growing cell is
    still unmeasured.**
  - **Grow now** (v1.123, owner: *"is there a exploit that we can make the croops
    grow instalntly"*) — the instant-growth question, asked of the four things
    that were never measured. It (0) walks `Modules` for a `ModuleScript` named
    **grow / crop / config** and requires each inside `pcall`, printing the table
    one level deep (v1.124, because the v1.123 remote list names
    `EVTUpdateCropGrowthConfig` — a broadcast config means a client copy may
    exist, and the per-crop **grow time** is the number both this question and the
    owner's order-ranking rule need); (1) prints **every stage name on one of our
    fields with the cell's own `PlantGrowth` / `GrowthMultiplier` / `IsWatered` /
    `SeedType`**, which is also the only way to see whether the harvest job's
    "any stage we do not know is a standing crop" rule is being handed an
    immature name; (2) **gets INTO the harvester** (spawned out of storage if it
    is parked — the Tool-apart answer says a loose implement takes nothing, so a
    refusal read from outside the seat would be a false answer), parks the blade
    on a **Seeded** cell with the grid's own settle-poll and fires
    `FNCHarvestFieldCell` at it, then reads the **stage and the tank**: a take
    that raises `Capacity` is the exploit (the wait can be skipped), a take that
    raises nothing is a bare cut, a refusal means the server wants a ripe cell;
    (3) writes the cell's `PlantGrowth` to the **ripe number the game itself
    just printed** (never tried — Force a tile forged `Stage`) and distinguishes
    "a fresh `GetCells` dropped the write" (the cell table is a copy, nothing to
    patch) from "the mirror now reads ripe" (re-fire the cut and see whether the
    server consults it); (4) fires `FNCSprayFieldCell` with the **sprayer's own**
    attachment at that cell — neither the v1.66 run (an *Empty* tile) nor the
    v1.123 one (a `Seeded` tile, but `(tool none)` — the call carried **no**
    attachment) measured water-vs-growth on a growing crop — and finds the
    sprayer by walking `Objects.Equipment` for a spray/sprinkler/water/irrig
    name, printing every machine with its real `EquipmentType` when none
    matches (no guessed type name). It is **not** in Run all, which stays the two
    read-only probes; this one sits in a machine and cuts a cell. No harness —
    the live re-read is the verdict, and `Copy report` carries it.
    **ANSWERED (the owner's run, v1.124):** `early cut — Seeded → Empty, tank
    0 → 1 — TAKEN AND IT PAID`, so the wait is skippable and the harvest call
    does **not** want a ripe crop. The behaviour is §3.14 (**Early harvest**,
    on by default from v1.125); the same run named the working attachment
    (`HarvesterAxle.AxleAttachment` — the SRC6 has no `RaycastPart`, so the
    harvest-sounding rank wins), read `CropGrowthConfigClient → GetConfig=fn
    SetConfig=fn`, and showed **no sprayer on this plot**, so steps (4)'s water
    question cannot be answered here at all.
  **Copy report** (`spudTestCopy`) now also appends the last **60 farm-log
  lines** from `_G.SPUD_TRACE` (v1.85) — a Tests-only copy cannot answer "why is
  harvesting silent", and the console is the place the owner does not want to
  dig in. **Copy console** (`spudTestCopyConsole`, v1.86) is the same clipboard
  without the test run — the last **150** farm-log lines — for a run that is
  stalling and needs no experiment (owner: *"you should add a copy consol line
  bro"*).
  **Run all** runs the two remaining probes (remote list, field remotes); both
  refuse while `loopThread` is live and touch no animals or other plots.
  There is **no harness** for them — the live read is the only verdict.
  **Removed once answered** (v1.44–v1.80; every result is in §5 and the console):
  From here / Wrong stage / No attachment / All at once (seat, stage and
  attachment are checked, and a multi-id list is only partly taken), Sell abuse
  (`EVTSellAtStore` ignores distance; an empty tank never pays twice), Grab from
  afar (carry works at 35 studs), Spawn from afar (works from anywhere), Fill
  from afar (refused at 963 studs), Field API (the module exports **no setter**;
  `UpdateCell`/`UpdateCells` are the client's own writers), Force a tile,
  **Wrong tool** (v1.69): a plow's own attachment firing cultivate on a Plowed
  cell the blade had been hopped onto stayed `Plowed → Plowed — IGNORED`, so
  the server checks *which implement* the attachment belongs to — one sweep
  cannot run every stage, and each verb keeps its own tool — and **Lift test**
  (v1.74): three runs returned the same band, a call TAKEN with the blade
  **20 studs to the side at +8** (30 refused) and from **+80 studs straight
  above**, so the sweep's 20-stud reach and rows are measured, not guessed, and
  **Tool apart** (v1.80): a plow taken off the tractor with its blade **0.0
  studs** from an Empty cell still came back `IGNORED`, so a field call also
  needs the implement on the ride the player is driving — position is not the
  only check, and a loose tool can never be used.

**Remote inventory (v1.60, owner's report 2026-10-30).** 334 remotes under
`ReplicatedStorage`, all on `Modules.Larry`. Field: `EVTPlowFieldCell`,
`EVTCultivateFieldCell`, `FNCSeedFieldCell`, `FNCHarvestFieldCell`,
`FNCSprayFieldCell`, `EVTFieldPacket`, `FNCEditField`,
`FNCConvertRundownField`. **Never wired, and now named**: `EVTHerdRequest`,
`EVTAnimalAction`, `EVTCollectAnimalProduction`, `EVTFilthCleaning`,
`EVTFillFeeder`, `EVTFillFeedDispenser`, `EVTOpenBarnGate`, `EVTToggleDoor`,
`EVTDepositWarehouse`, `EVTWithdrawWarehouse`, `EVTFulfillOrder`,
`EVTTeleportToFarm`, `FNCCutTree`/`EVTToppleTree`, `EVTSellObjects`,
`EVTSellAtAnimalCo`, `FNCForceRespawnEquipment`, `FNCSetEquipmentHome`. Those
are the next candidates for the milk/barn/feed/gate/deposit steps that still
press keys or drive the UI.

**v1.124 re-read (the 339 that came back after the cap was raised).** Same
`Modules.Larry` home, and it names two doors the open questions were waiting on.
**Growth**: `EVTUpdateCropGrowthConfig` (the server broadcasts a crop-growth
config — so a client copy may be readable, which is what **Grow now**'s config
walk asks), plus `EVTWatered`, `EVTHireCropduster` / `EVTHireFreeCropDuster` /
`EVTUsedFreeCropDuster` (a hired duster waters a whole field — never probed) and
`EVTSendOfflineGrowthSummary`. **Company orders** (task #6, and this is the first
time the game has named them): `FNCGetOrderProgress`, `FNCAcceptOrder`,
`EVTRefreshOrders` / `EVTFreeRefreshOrders`, `EVTOrderDepositProgress`,
`EVTOrderFulfilledFX`, `EVTWithdrawOrderFX`, `EVTDepositIntoTrainCar`,
`EVTTrainOrderDeposited`, `EVTCompleteTask` / `EVTLoadNewTasks` / `EVTRefillTasks`
/ `EVTSendTasksToPlayer` / `EVTUpdateTasks` / `FNCSelectGoal`, and
`FNCGetAvailableBoxes`. None is wired and none has been fired — **rule 4 still
applies: the arguments are unknown until a probe reads them**, which is what
`1000054.lua` was built to answer.

**v1.138 re-read (the owner's `Run all`, 2026-11-03).** `342 found … (3004 nodes
walked, CAP 3000 REACHED — this list is cut short)`, so the tail past the last
printed name (`FNCUpdateStructure`) is still unread — honest, and not a blocker,
because every door the order feature needs is in the printed part **with its
class now**: `FNCGetOrderProgress (RemoteFunction)`, `FNCGetAvailableBoxes
(RemoteFunction)` and `FNCAcceptOrder (RemoteFunction)`. That means the new
probe's `ClassName == "RemoteFunction"` gate will really fire its two getters,
and that `FNCAcceptOrder` is a function rather than an event — the one door the
leg refuses to touch. The task/goal family is all present (`EVTCompleteTask`,
`EVTLoadNewTasks`, `EVTRefillTasks`, `EVTSendTasksToPlayer`, `EVTUpdateTasks`,
`FNCSelectGoal`), and the deposit/factory doors are named here for the first
time (`EVTDepositProduce`, `EVTDepositOntoInputStack`, `EVTGrabFromOutputStack`,
`EVTTakeFromOutputStack`, `EVTHarvesterPipeDump`, `EVTWarehouseDepositAnimation`,
`EVTTransferCropsFromSilo`, `EVTPullFactoryLever`, `EVTRunFactoryPistons`,
`EVTJuicery.*`, `EVTSawmill.*`, `EVTSpindle.*`). None is wired (rule 16); that
list is what the factory question will need next. The same report also says
`(tool none)` — no implement in `Objects.Equipment` at all right now — and its
log tail is `milk store: (no trailer or silo found)` five times, so neither the
sprayer question nor v1.137's liquid-deck sale has anything to read against on
this client yet.

**Is a tile forceable?** No — and the last candidate is closed too.
`EVTFieldPacket` is the server telling clients which cells changed: live packets
are a **4-byte header + 6-byte cell records** (`CellToBuffer` is the 6 bytes; the
stage is one byte — live `04` Cultivated, `08` Plowed, `0c` Seeded, `20` on a
standing crop). Forging one was tested twice: a bare record (v1.62) and then a
**real** packet with only its stage byte patched (v1.64) — both ended with **no
packet back and no cell moved**, so there is no client→server handler. The live
dump also shows no setter, and Force a tile (2026-10-30) ended `the client cell table is writable — UpdateCell kept the
forged Cultivated for 2 s` then `reads Cultivated — IGNORED: no update arrived`:
the server never reads the client's copy. A cell only holds `Alternate,
GrowthMultiplier, IsWatered, PlantGrowth, Position, SeedType, Stage` (50 cells,
`Stage=Plowed` on the live field). `spudSweepCells` therefore forces *position*,
not state — and position has far more slack than assumed. Measured live
(2026-10-31, every reading retried): a cell was TAKEN with the blade **20
studs to the side at +8** (30 refused) and with the blade **+80 studs straight
above** it, so the check is the ground under the blade within ~2 tiles — not
that it is over the cell — and a parked blade clears the block around it
(`spudRadialFire`, v1.71). The three untouched field calls are
readers/no-ops live (`FNCSprayFieldCell` changes nothing, `FNCEditField()`
returns a table, `FNCConvertRundownField()` wants an instance) and the **tool
identity is checked** (cultivate fired from a plow's own attachment at a
Plowed cell the blade was over stayed `Plowed → Plowed — IGNORED`), so one
sweep cannot run every stage.

`1000016.py` pins the ids, the attachment pick, the call shape, the
full/partial/ignored outcomes, the session switch-off, the fill fallback, the
hop, the sweep (a 15-cell field taken in one tween, the budget cutting it
short, and an ignored sweep leaving every cell standing) and the single-spot
fire (a 60-stud reach taking all 15 cells from one teleport and 15 calls, a
4-seed seeder firing 4 and handing back dry, a 5-stud reach taking nothing).

### 3.13 Equipment actions fire the captured calls

User: *"use remoted for everything."* The same probe pass recorded the
equipment/action calls, so the loop now fires them instead of pressing keys or
driving the Equipment Manager UI. All live under `Modules.Larry`:

```
FNCEnterEquipment(vehicle, seat)
FNCAttemptHitch(tractor.Components.Hitchable, tool.Chassis.HitchableSocket)
FNCUnhitch(tractor.Components.Hitchable)
FNCSpawnEquipment(model) / FNCStoreEquipment(model)
EVTRequestToCarry(draggable) / EVTRequestToDrop()
```

- **Fired only where the game offers the action**: mount after the walk-up,
  hitch/unhitch at the same line-up pose the key needs, carry at the crate,
  spawn/store for a machine that is actually stored/out.
  **A carry is asked for from *beside* the box** (v1.143, live): all three
  `carryCrate` call sites teleport the character **onto** the crate, and §5
  v1.108 already measured what that does — a hop onto a draggable throws the
  player off it — so the `Carry` prompt never armed and the run kept buying; the
  live refusals read `EnterEquipment`, `Drop`, `ToggleDoor` and plain `nothing`,
  while a carry that does land says
  `✅ carrying the seed crate (E, attempt 1)`. `carryCrate` now moves to
  `spudCarryNear = 8` studs off the crate's own part (along the character→crate
  line, so it is a step sideways, never a second mount), waits three frames
  (§11), and reads `contextActions()` before pressing: a readable list with no
  `Carry` in it prints `standing 8 studs off the <what> offers no Carry — the
  game offers: <verbatim> — pressing anyway`. `spudOfferSaysCarry` answers
  `true` / `false` / **`nil`**, and only `false` logs — an unreadable read still
  presses (rule 7: no verdict dressed up as a refusal, and no refusal dressed up
  as a bug). **And a crate that never came to hand is a failed trip**: the pour
  path used to `return true` after `carryCrate` reported the grab dead, so four
  shop trips burned while the run said the seeder was loaded; it now logs
  `the crate didn't come to hand — nothing was poured` and returns false.
- **The effect is the proof** — the seat holding the humanoid
  (`spudEnterRemote`), the existing hitch/unhitch tug (`attemptHitch`,
  `dropImplementAtOrigin`), `InStorage` flipping (or a >100-stud move for a
  spawn that rebuilds the model) in `spudEquipRemoteAct`, and `carrying()`
  turning over for carry/drop.
- **Sell and buy** — `EVTSellAtStore(ride, pad)` is fired for the **Sell**
  destination (the tank dropping is the proof; Q/E after a 3 s grace), and
  `FNCRequestStorePurchase({crate})` is fired at the buy popup (balance or a
  new crate is the proof; the menu click is the fallback). **Fulfillment**
  makes no call and presses no key: the pad unloads a parked harvester itself
  (v1.41), so the trip only waits on the tank.
- **The buy clicks a control for what its LABEL says** (v1.141, live). The dialog
  reader used to join `guiTextOf(btn)` and `btn.Name` into one string and match
  words against the join — and `guiTextOf` **falls back to the instance Name**
  when a control has no readable text, so a name was always in the string. The
  live console named what that bought:

  ```
  seed shop: clicking Players.ok_simba3.PlayerGui.GuiViews.HUD.Notifications.ConfirmAction.Content.ConfirmButton ('Delete Farm') — the Purchase button
  seed shop: the menu is still showing after 267 visible control(s) — nothing bought.
  ```

  `ConfirmButton` is an `Action` confirm whose own `Content` label reads
  **`Delete Farm`** — a notification about the farm, not the seed till — and the
  old `forbidden` list held no destructive word, while a third tier clicked "the
  first usable control" when nothing buy-shaped matched. Three rules, and they are
  the only shop-click rules now: **(1)** a **danger** word
  (`spudShopDangerWords`: robux / premium / gems / gem / sell / cancel / close /
  back / exit / delete / destroy / remove / reset / unsubscribe / leave) in
  **either** half refuses the click — reading a label is not permission to be
  destructive, and the name is on the path for a reason; **(2)** a **buy** word
  (`spudShopBuyChoice`: purchase / buy / confirm / checkout) counts in the
  **label**, and the Name is consulted only when the label *cannot* speak — that
  is `label == name`, the case `guiTextOf` returns the Name because the control is
  drawn as an image (the game's green Purchase button really is one, §5 v1.113);
  **(3)** there is **no arbitrary-click tier**. A buy-shaped control that is also
  dangerous is logged (`a buy-shaped control was REFUSED, not clicked — …`) rather
  than dropped quietly, and when nothing reads as a buy the run says
  `nothing on screen reads as a buy of Wheat (267 visible button(s): …) —
  clicking nothing` and stops. The same two predicates drive the **Shop counter**
  probe, so a probe can no longer click what the farm refuses to; the words live
  in **globals** (`spudShopDanger` / `spudShopBuyChoice` / `spudShopDangerWords`)
  for the 200-local reason and because the harness slices and runs them directly
  (`tools/1000034.py`, the two predicates plus the picker and the real
  tier block run for real, and the source ties refuse a regression). `shownPurchase()` still reads
  the joined text — it only *locates* the dialog container to close and never
  clicks what it finds.
  **A label is not a till** (v1.143, live). Those three rules stopped the
  destructive press and then bought **nothing anyway**, because the loop took
  the *first* buy-shaped control and `break`ed:

  ```
  seed shop: clicking Players.ok_simba3.PlayerGui.GuiViews.Stores.PalletStoreMenu.Main.Border.Content.InfoPanel.PurchaseButton ('Buy') — the Purchase button
  seed shop: balance 1519251 -> 1519251
  ⚠ the Rice purchase could NOT be confirmed (toast: "You need to own the Forklift License!")
  ```

  That is §5 v1.107's **pallet** till — buy-shaped, not dangerous, and it sorts
  ahead of the seed dialog. A fourth rule, and it is a **path** rule because the
  path is the only thing that names which till a control belongs to:
  `spudShopBuyPick(cands)` collects **every** safe buy-shaped candidate (each
  carrying its own lowercased `GetFullName()`), passes over one whose path says
  `pallet` or `forklift` in favour of any other, and if the till is all there is
  it clicks **nothing** and prints which till it refused. Both words come from
  that console line and that toast — no invented third word, because an invented
  one would refuse the real buy (rule 4).
  **The close judge reads the same rule** (v1.144, live). `shownPurchase()` is
  not only how the trip finds what to click — it is also how it answers *"did
  the dialog shut?"*, and it returned the **first** purchase-shaped control in
  all of `GuiViews`. This town keeps the pallet till's `PurchaseButton` on
  screen, so a seed dialog that had closed was still reported
  `Stores is still open after handler then again — close it by hand once`, the
  close step chased the till's container, and the second crate was bought
  against a till that was never shut. The judge now gathers every
  purchase-shaped control with its full path and hands the list to
  `spudShopBuyPick`, so one rule covers the click, the probe and the verdict.
- **The shop closes itself after a buy** (v1.96, owner 2026-10-31: *"after it
  buys a seed can you make it close the ui"*). The old close step searched the
  whole `GuiViews` tree for a "close" button and then pressed F whenever **any**
  GuiButton was on screen — the HUD and top bar always are — so F fired every
  time and the shop menu stayed up. `buySeedAndFill` now reads the actual open
  menus (`GuiViews.Menus` plus any `*Menu` layer beside it, by
  `LayerCollector.Enabled`), clicks that menu's own close control **inside** it
  (text/name close/exit/back/cancel/dismiss, or an `X`), re-reads `Enabled`, and
  only presses F if it is still up. It only closes what the trip **raised**: the Enabled layer set is
  recorded just before F, so the always-enabled layers (`SettingsMenu`,
  `BuildMode`, `FarmerPass`, `PalletStorage`…) are never touched (v1.99 — the
  first cut tried to close those and only logged warnings). The buy dialog is
  closed **by its own container** (v1.100): that screen is a frame inside an
  always-enabled layer, so Enabled alone reported "no shop menu is open" while
  the dialog sat on screen — a shown **Purchase/Buy** control now names the
  container GuiViews holds it in, and that container's own close (the red X) is
  clicked before the layer pass runs. Every run logs
  `seed shop: <Menu> closed (handler)` or the honest
  `<Menu> is still open after F — close it by hand once`; a run with nothing
  new open says `seed shop: no shop menu is open — nothing to close`.
- **The old path always remains**: F for a mount, X for hitch/unhitch, E for a
  crate, the Equipment Manager UI for spawn/store. `spudEquipRemoteMiss` counts
  ignored calls; three switch that one action back to keys/UI for the session.
- **Not wired yet**: cleaning a stall, milking, herding, feeders and gates.
  Their shapes were never
  captured — `1000055.lua` now has an **ACTION CALLS** section and a
  checklist for them (run it, or let one normal milk/barn cycle record while it
  runs), and `EQUIPMENT CALLS` confirms those above.

- **The manager's card list is filtered to the tab the game last showed**
  (v1.135, live 2026-11-03 — the run that could never get its ride back).
  `GuiViews.Menus.GarageMenu.Background.Content.Cards` only holds the cards of
  the **active tab**, and the tabs are the game's own words:
  `All · Tractor · Harvester · Plow · Cultivator · Planter · Truck · Transport ·
  Misc`. Chasing `ChallengerMT9` — a Tractor — the list read exactly two cards,
  `KADL · Tempo L16`, both Planters, because the pass before it was working on
  the seeder; five attempts, `wouldn't drive for this vehicle`, run ended. And
  because a farming pass **stores the ride at the end of every pass**, that is a
  self-made lock: the next pass could only ever see the implement's tab. So a
  card miss now asks the game to show everything — it finds the `Tabs` child
  whose folded name is `all` and drives it through `clickEffect` (the game's own
  connected handlers, then `:Activate()`; **no mouse**, per the owner's rule),
  polls for the card, and logs `the <machine> card was under another tab — the
  game's All tab showed it`. A build with no such tab is named with its child
  count instead of failing silently.
- **A card NAME is not an identity** (v1.144, live). The same diagnostic
  printed `6 card(s) showing right now: Tempo L16 · JD Shank · Horsch Joker ·
  JD S790 · Tempo L16 · Challenger MT9` — **two** cards named `Tempo L16`,
  because this farm owns two of the planter. `findCard` returned the first
  match, `storeOne` retried it five times, and the planter that was actually
  out stayed out: `couldn't store the TempoL16 after 5 tries`, twice in one
  seed pass, each pass paying five open-the-garage/click-Store/close cycles.
  `findCard` now gathers **every** card carrying the name and answers the
  `which`-th (wrapping), `managerAction(name, action, which)` passes the
  attempt number, and `storeOne` hands it `attempt` — so five tries cannot all
  click the twin that was already stored. The final refusal names what it
  still holds (in the world or gone, its own `InStorage` read, plus
  `spudEquipHereText()`), because which of the two cards is the out machine is
  not something this build has measured (rule 4).
- **A fall-through names which half broke** (v1.135): `spudEquipRemoteAct` used
  to `return false` in three places with no log at all, so a console could not
  tell "no remote in this build", "the call threw", "switched off for the
  session", "the machine is not in `Objects.Equipment`" and "it already reads
  the state you asked for". Each now prints its own line (with the folder's
  child count on the missing-model one) before the Equipment Manager runs, and
  the machine lookup folds the name the same way, so a label like `Tempo L16`
  finds the model `TempoL16` instead of silently falling to the UI.

`1000010.py` pins the kit, the call shapes, the effect proofs, the
UI/key fallbacks, the three-miss switch-off, the folded model lookup, all five
named fall-throughs and (by ties) the `All`-tab rescue sitting before the
`no card` bail.

### 3.14 A crop does not have to be ripe to be cut (the early harvest)

**Answered live by the owner's own run of Tests → Grow now (v1.124):**

```
test: growth: early cut — Seeded → Empty, tank 0 → 1 — TAKEN AND IT PAID:
a crop can be harvested before it is ripe — the wait can be skipped
```

So the growth wait is optional, and the farm loop now takes it. Global
`spudEarlyCut` (**on** by default, remembered in `spudSavedEarlyCut`, Settings
toggle **Early harvest**) makes a `Seeded` cell count as a standing crop in the
**one** predicate every harvest decision reads — `grownStage(s)`, used by
`whichNeed(JOBS[4])`, `whichCells`, `spudFieldCellIds` and the per-field census —
so the tour, the cell ids, the tank gate and the wait cannot disagree about what
"cuttable" means. A cycle is then: plow → cultivate → seed → harvest (the same
field, minutes later) → sell → replant, and an hour of standing in
`🌱 growing: 50/50 cell(s) still Seeded` becomes one restart line,
`🌱 early cut: 50 cell(s) are cuttable now — restarting the loop instead of
waiting`.

Three things this deliberately does **not** change:

- **Off means the old build.** With the toggle off, `Seeded` is again not
  standing, the wait waits, and the census column reads `standing` instead of
  `cuttable` — `1000007.py` pins both halves, plus that the plow,
  cultivate and seed jobs aim at their own stage name either way.
- **Unreadable is still not empty** (rule 7): a nil read or a `total = 0` read
  returns `nil` from `whichNeed`, so no pick turns an unreadable field into a
  finished one. (`whichNeed` used to compute `total − the four known names`,
  which threw on a table with no `total`; it now sums the stage names through
  the predicate, which cannot.)
- **The restart is bounded.** `spudCutWatch` (a global table) remembers the last
  cuttable count: the **same number twice in a row** means the harvest pass took
  nothing — no harvester on a picked foreign farm, a refused call — so from the
  third read on `waitForGrowth` waits like a normal cycle instead of restarting
  on a dime and hammering `GetCells` in a hot loop.

The attachment that made this measurable is also the one the loop now uses:
`spudToolAttachment(SRC6)` ranks **RaycastPart → harvest-sounding part →
Seed/Detect → any**, and the SRC6 has no `RaycastPart` at all, so it picks
`HarvesterAxle.AxleAttachment` (the probe prints `attachment: SRC6 →
HarvesterAxle.AxleAttachment (harvest)`). That is the v1.89 wheel-strut failure
closed with a live take, not with another guess.

**Get coords** (Settings, 📍) exists because the pad positions this build parks
on came out of the owner standing somewhere and typing the numbers into the chat.
It reads `HumanoidRootPart.Position`, logs `(x, y, z)` rounded to whole studs and
puts the same text on the clipboard (`coords: (…) — copied, paste it in the
chat`, or the honest `the copy was refused, read it above`). Stand on a pad, press
it, paste it here: that is how a spot the script cannot stream becomes a spot it
can drive to. Its first four readings (2026-11-02) are the table in §3.10 — and
because a standing reading is the **deck**, not the part centre, it corrected the
Fulfillment pad's Y from the dump's 138 to 141.

### 3.15 The standing crop is the one the owner is planting

`SeedType` on a live field cell reads the game's own word (`Tomato` — the v1.124
`Grow now` census printed `Seeded ×50 [growth 2012, mult 1, watered false, seed
Tomato]`), so the Farm tab's seed default now looks for that word in
`FieldHelper.SEED_TYPE` through `spudNameKey` (`seed default: Tomato (the crop
you are planting)`) — owner's 2026-11-03: *"btw we are planting tomatoes so mke
it plnt tht"*. Order of precedence, and none of it is a name this build invented:

1. the **remembered** Farm-tab pick (`spudSavedSeed`, only while the game still
   offers that name) — a hand-picked seed always wins over a default;
2. **Tomato**, matched by folded name in whatever list this build exposes (so
   `Tomato`, `tomato` and a spaced or punctuated variant are one crop);
3. the game's **first crop** (`bestName` — the lowest `SEED_TYPE` id, the
   existing rule that replaced the alphabetical first, which was an unsellable
   event seed);
4. `list[1]`.

The `seed default:` line says which of those won, and a build whose list has no
Tomato falls through to the old behaviour unchanged.

The same read is what bounds the seed **round** (v1.135, the other half of the
owner's "seeding is bugging"): live, `seedPasses` ran rounds 10, 11 and 12 on one
field, each buying or pouring a 50-seed load and taking **nothing**, because the
calls it fired were not landing and nothing in the loop ever noticed a round that
changed no count. Two rounds that read `after >= leftWas` now end the pass —
`seeding: N cell(s) still need seed after 2 round(s) that took nothing — leaving
them for the next pass (the hitch and tool lines above say why)` — and a refill
resets the count, so a field that is genuinely progressing is never cut off. The
end is a decision made on a **count**, not a guess, and the cells are left
standing for the next pass rather than eaten by an infinite loop.

### 3.16 One session, one build: the token lives in the shared env

Every long-lived thread asks `spudAlive()` and stops when the menu that started
it is gone. That is the whole Unload/Reload story (§5, v1.24) — and until v1.136
the counter it compared against was a **bare global**, which is only as shared as
the environment the executor happened to run the file in. The emulator proved what
that costs (owner's build, live 2026-11-03, while a run sat on
`Equipment Manager: spawning ChallengerMT9`):

```
11-03 11:17:15.855 | … still working (Equipment Manager: spawning ChallengerMT9)
11-03 11:17:19.788 | … still working (Equipment Manager: spawning ChallengerMT9)
11-03 11:17:26.603 | fps 24 · …
11-03 11:17:28.044 | fps 24 · …
```

Two independent ~10 s heartbeat streams with a constant ~1.44 s offset, and two
independent 30 s fps streams: **two builds of the script were alive in the same
client at the same time** — an autostarted one and a hand-run one, each passing
its own token check, each driving the same tractor. That is also why the stall
looked unbounded: `beat` only cleared at the **end** of the run body, so a
superseded build printed "still working (spawning ChallengerMT9)" forever next to
the real one.

v1.136 puts the counter where the executor keeps it — `spudEnv = (getgenv and
getgenv()) or _G` (the same shared table the build already uses to expose
`spudDoctor`) — and then makes **every** stop-test consult it:

- `stopping()` and `milkStopping()` each start with `if not spudAlive() then
  return true end`. A ghost's own `loopThread`/`milkThread` still points at its
  own thread, so the thread id alone can never say "I am a ghost"; the token can.
  Folding it into those two functions gives it to the hundreds of call sites for
  free, in both loops and in the automatic delivery thread.
- The run heartbeat is `while beat and spudAlive()` (and the same guard inside),
  so a build that no longer owns the session has nothing to report.
- `destroyUI` bumps **the shared** counter (`local env = spudEnv or _G`), so
  Unload ends every loop across executions rather than only the ones reading this
  environment's copy.
- The guarded read **fails CLOSED**: `((spudEnv and spudEnv.spudGen) or 0) ==
  myGen`, so a clobbered `spudEnv` stops every loop instead of letting a build
  that cannot prove ownership keep driving.
- **`spudAlive` stays `local`.** A global one gets overwritten by the next build,
  and the old loop — looking it up by name — would read the new token as "alive".
- 🩺 DOCTOR prints `session N` and, when this closure does not own N, says
  `GHOST BUILD (a newer one owns the session)` — so two builds on one client are
  visible in a report, not only in logcat timing.

The 11-minute spawn stall itself is a **disconnect** (Roblox Error 273, *"Same
account launched experience from different device"*): while the client is off the
server, a blocking remote never resumes. Every wait on that path is bounded (3×8 s
respawn poll, 40×0.25 s card poll, 2×8×0.25 s click poll), so the honest reading of
a frozen stage line is "the client is not talking to the server", and the ghost
heartbeat is what made it look like a loop that never ends. Reconnecting is the
owner's call — it evicts the other device.

### 3.17 A full liquid carrier is driven to the deck that pays for it

The fourth town address in §3.10 — **sell liquid, `86, 141, -288`** — had no
consumer for eleven builds: the milk loop filled a carrier, the carrier filled
up, and the loop said `store: <carrier> is FULL (n/m) — empty it or pick another
carrier` and ended the pass. The owner's 2026-11-03 ask closes that half:
*"finnishe the milk loop"*. Now a full carrier is **sold**, and the pass carries
on filling the next jug against the fresh readout.

The rule is the same one v1.41/v1.122 established for the Fulfillment deck, and
nothing here invents a mechanism the game has not already shown (rule 4):

- **Only the character moves.** The trip is `milkTp(deck + (0,3,0))` — the same
  hop the deposit pass already uses to reach a trailer — and never a `PivotTo` on
  a loaded tanker, because a 600-unit carrier flung across town is the exact
  "falls out the map" history §3.12 is written around. The **machine** only moves
  through the game's own `managerAction(name, "Spawn")`, the same call the top of
  `milkStoreDeposit` already uses to bring a stored carrier out; a full carrier
  costs nothing to relocate, since it cannot accept a jug anyway.
- **The readout is the only proof.** `milkStoreAmount(dest)` dropping below what
  it read before the trip is the sale. A key press, a remote's return value and
  "the button lit up" are not proof, and none of them ends the trip.
- **No input is tried first.** The build has one measured precedent for a deck
  that drains a parked vehicle by itself (the Fulfillment pad), so the park is
  held for 8 s with **zero** presses. Only when the tank refuses to move does it
  read what the game actually renders — `milkStoreActs()` (the visible
  `PCGP_ContextActions` offers, not the whole ~70-entry registry) — and drives one
  whose own name says unload / sell / empty / offload / deposit through
  `milkStorePress`, i.e. the game's connected handlers, then `:Activate()`. **No
  mouse, no invented remote** — in particular `EVTSellAtStore` is *not* fired here,
  because its captured shape is `(ride, pad)` and this deck has no pad part to put
  in slot two; firing it at a measured position would be an invented use.
- **A band, logged so it can be tuned**: global `spudLiquidDeckNear = 45` — wider
  than `spudFulfillPadNear = 35` because a tanker is a long vehicle whose trigger
  runs along its body, and because no build has ever printed what this deck
  offers. The distance is measured flat (XZ), so the +3 Y of the hop cannot fake
  a miss.
- **Three misses switch the trip off** for the session (`milkLiquidOff`, counted
  by `milkLiquidTries`, reset by any sale and by every build — the same shape as
  `spudEquipRemoteMiss`), because a sale that never lands is a town teleport and
  an 8 s wait, once per pass, forever.
- **A silo is refused outright** (its `EquipmentType` says silo, or it lives in
  `Objects.Buildings`): it has no wheels and no Garage card, so the only way to
  "drive it to the deck" is a teleport that would strand a building. The refusal
  is rate-limited to one line per 120 s.
- **Every exit returns to the plot.** `home()` hops the character back within 3
  frames, and it is declared *before* `noSale()` (rule 14 — both are locals of the
  trip's own prototype, and the later one calls the earlier). Without it the
  automatic delivery thread would sit in town reading
  `no milk jugs waiting at this plot` and go idle far from its own barn.

The failure line is the probe: a sale that does not happen prints
`liquid deck: <carrier> still reads <n>/<cap> after being parked there — what the
spot offers: <verbatim list>`, so the first run that cannot sell is the run that
names the deck's real offers — which is the number this feature had to ship
without guessing.

Deliberately **not** built (rule 16): no mid-loop trip to town while jugs are
still filling, no spawn-back of the carrier after the sale, and no drain attempt
for a carrier that is only partly full (there is nothing to sell, and a
half-empty tanker is still a valid destination for the next jug).

### 3.18 The Company-order questions are asked from INSIDE the build

The auto-contract feature is approved (owner 2026-11-03: *"you did say smth about
making the auto contracs thigns, i want that tho"*) and its rules are on the
record — **crops only**, pick the **easiest** order by **seeding time and cost**,
*"if it dont got enough money to buy 1 single seed mox it skips and goes to a
lower cost one"*, **only steer the next planting**, unload anything already in the
tank **before** planting again, and *"try 5 more times then go to sell"*. What it
cannot be written against is the data: **no dump has ever named the instance an
order lives in**, and **no run has ever printed a per-crop grow time** (rule 4).
`1000054.lua` was written to answer that and has never been run — and
running it means putting a **second script** in the executor, which is exactly how
the v1.136 ghost build happened (two builds of one client driving one tractor).

So the questions moved into the shipped build: **Tests → Company orders**
(`spudTestWork("orders")`). Every line it prints goes through `spudTestSay` →
`log()` → Roblox's CreatorOutput → **Android logcat**, so the report is readable
with the single autostarted build running and no second executor tab. It answers,
in order:

0. **The client's own order tables** (v1.149) — `ReplicatedStorage` is walked
   to depth 3 for a `ModuleScript` whose name says order / contract / task / goal
   / box / quota / cargo / shipment / fulfill, each `require`d inside `pcall` and
   dumped two levels (`key={sub-keys}`, `fn` for a function, the value for a
   scalar, `+N more` past fourteen). This is the section the payload question
   actually needs: sections 1 and 4 answer WHERE an order is and WHAT IS OPEN,
   and neither names a field. The grow-time answer came from exactly such a
   module (`CropGrowthConfigClient`), so asking the game's own loader is the
   measured way to learn an order's shape instead of inventing one (rule 4). No
   remote is fired here — a `require` runs the client's own loader and nothing
   else — and a module that wants an argument prints its refusal text.
1. **Where** anything order-shaped is, by name (`order / contract / request /
   demand / company / cargo / freight / shipment / delivery / goal / task /
   fulfill / reward / quota`, folded through `spudNameKey` so the game's own
   spacing is not a special case). It walks `workspace` to depth 8 with a 20 000-node
   budget, **recursing whether or not a node matched** — an order is normally the
   *child* of an order-named folder, so stopping at the parent would hide the
   shape the probe exists to report — and prints `CAP 20000 REACHED — this is not
   the whole map` when it runs out (the v1.123 truncated-remote-list mistake, not
   repeated). Zero matches is reported as *"that is not "no orders", it means the
   board is elsewhere"* (rule 7), never as an empty game.
2. **What** each hit holds: path, class, which word matched, its attributes, and
   up to 16 descendants with their values (`ObjectValue`s print their target,
   because an order that points at a field is a different reader than one that
   points at nothing), plus the honest `more descendants on this one` and
   `… N more order-named instance(s) not shown`.
3. **The words the player can read** — `PlayerGui` is walked too, because a
   `ScreenGui` board is invisible to a workspace-only walk. A label counts when it
   says an order word **or** sits under an order-named instance; nothing found is
   named as *"the board is not on screen from here"*.
4. **The doors** — every `Modules.Larry` instance whose name says order / task /
   goal / box / fulfill / contract, with its **class**, so `FNCGetOrderProgress
   [RemoteFunction]` and `EVTRefreshOrders [RemoteEvent]` are never confused again.
5. **A getter only.** The single deliberate exception to "a probe fires nothing",
   and bounded by the repo's own precedent (`FNCEditField()` is invoked just to
   read the shape it demands): an **allow-list** of two names —
   `FNCGetOrderProgress` and `FNCGetAvailableBoxes` — and only when that name is
   actually a `RemoteFunction` in this build. **No `EVT*`, no `FNCAccept*` is
   ever called**, and the call is bracketed by `coinBalance()` before and after,
   so a getter that moves the wallet prints `⚠ the balance MOVED on a getter —
   treat every order call as a write` in the same report rather than being
   discovered by the owner's balance. A refusal prints its **error text**, because
   `attempt to index nil with 'Name'` names the argument the reader has to pass
   (`FNCConvertRundownField` taught that trick); an empty table is called an
   answer, not a failure.
6. **The ranking key** — a grow time, asked of the game's own config module
   (anything in `Modules` named grow / crop / config, required inside `pcall`),
   reading both shapes: a per-crop table whose key says grow/time, and a `Get…`
   function called with no arguments. If neither is there it says
   `NO client-side grow time is readable — the ranking would have to run on cost
   alone`, which is the answer that changes the feature's design rather than a
   number invented to keep it comfortable. The remembered `spudSeedPrices` entries
   come back too — the **cost** half of the ranking is already measurable from the
   shop trips.

`tools/1000030.py` runs the **real** leg (sliced out of the file) under
`luau.exe` against three fake worlds: one that has orders (folder tree + a
`ScreenGui` board + the door set + a readable grow config), one that has nothing
order-named anywhere, and one whose getter **takes money**. Every fixture instance
carries a counting `FireServer` / `InvokeServer` / `PivotTo`, so "nothing was
fired" is asserted as `fired=0 … keys=0 moved=0` rather than as a reading of the
source, and the eight source ties pin the allow-list literally excludes
`FNCAcceptOrder` and `EVTRefreshOrders`.

**ANSWERED live (the assistant's MuMu press, v1.139).** Steps 5 and 6 came back:
`FNCGetOrderProgress()` and `FNCGetAvailableBoxes()` are both invoked with **no
arguments** and neither moves the wallet (`coins 923235 → 923235`); one answers an
**empty table** (a real answer — no order open from where the character stands) and
the other a table whose numeric key holds **1232**, so the shape is
`{ [<id>] = <number> }`. The grow-time question has a **yes**: the game's own
`CropGrowthConfigClient` gives one number per crop — **`Tomato = 2178`**, `Wheat =
45`, `Rice = 480`, `Soybean = 2178`, `Grape = 2400`, `Balsam = 1500`, `Cypress =
2280`, `Holly = 1800`, `Mint = 1800`, `Alien Tentacle = 3660`, `Steve = 600`,
`Stunted Hallowood = 1200` — plus a `Configs` table with `RSVPEvents` / `Stories`,
and no shelf price had been read that build (a seed trip fills `spudSeedPrices`,
the **cost** half of the ranking). Steps 1–3 are still empty and the probe says so
honestly: `0 instance(s) … named like an order (20004 node(s) walked, CAP 20000
REACHED — this is not the whole map)` and 386 `PlayerGui` label hits, none of them
order-shaped, so **the board is not in the world and not on screen from the field**
— it lives behind a menu this probe will not click (§6 item 7).
