old `empty it` line surviving as the else — so the honest message is still what a
third failed trip says.

Compile OK, suite **36/36** (33 harnesses + 3 gates), headroom **5** (unchanged —
the trip is a 4-space `function`, i.e. a global, beside two new globals, and all
five of its helpers are locals of its own prototype; rule 13 is why none of them
is a build-closure `local`), name audit clean **380**. **Harness green — not
reloaded in game**, and the first liquid-full pass is the verdict: either
`✅ liquid sold at the deck — Milk Tanker 600 → 0, coins 14459 → 15231 (parked,
pressing nothing)` and the pass carries on filling, or
`liquid deck: … still reads 600/600 after being parked there — what the spot
offers: …`, which names the action the trip should have pressed. A third line
would be `the spawn left … 412 studs from the deck`, which means the deck is
right but the carrier will not travel by the Garage, and the fix then is the
character pushing the machine — not this trip.

**"you did say smth about making the auto contracs thigns, i want that tho"
(v1.138).** The feature is approved in five rules (owner, 2026-11-02, recorded in
§5's v1.121 entry): **crops only**; choose the **easiest** order by **seeding
time and cost**; *"if it dont got enough money to buy 1 single seed mox it skips
and goes to a lower cost one"*; **only steer the next planting** — never rip out
what is already growing; and *"it should put the tihngs or sell them before
planting again bro"*. None of it can be written yet, and the reason is the
owner's own rule 4: **no dump this repo has ever read has named a Company order
instance**, and **no run has ever printed a crop grow time**, so the reader would
be a guess wearing a feature. The probe that answers those two questions existed
already — `1000054.lua`, 1,000 read-only lines, harness green since the
v1.121 work — but running it means **a second script in the executor**, which is
precisely how the v1.136 ghost build happened (two builds driving one tractor, §3.16).

So v1.138 moves the questions **into the shipped build**: a sixth `kind` in
`spudTestWork` and a **Company orders** button on the Tests tab (§3.18). That
changes nothing about the farm and everything about how the answer gets here —
every `spudTestSay` line ends in `log("test: " …)`, so the report is in Android's
logcat and readable with the **one** autostarted build. Six sections, all of them
read-only: the whole map walked for a name that says order / contract / request /
company / cargo / shipment / goal / task / fulfill / reward / quota (depth 8,
**CAP 20000 named when it trips**), each hit dumped with its attributes and up to
16 descendants **with their values**, the same walk over `PlayerGui` (a ScreenGui
board is invisible to a workspace-only walk — the mistake that made the standalone
probe's first version report nothing), the `Modules.Larry` door list **with each
door's class**, a grow-time read from the game's own config module, and the
remembered `spudSeedPrices` (the cost half of the ranking is already measurable
from the shop trips).

**The one deliberate exception to "a probe fires nothing"** is step 5 of §3.18: an
allow-list of exactly two names — `FNCGetOrderProgress` and
`FNCGetAvailableBoxes` — invoked only when that name is really a `RemoteFunction`
in this build. It is bounded by the repo's own precedent (`FNCEditField()` and
`FNCConvertRundownField()` are already invoked with no arguments purely to read
the shape they demand), it never touches an `EVT*` or `FNCAccept*`, and it is
**bracketed by `coinBalance()` before and after**: a getter that moves the wallet
prints `⚠ the balance MOVED on a getter — treat every order call as a write` in
the same report rather than being discovered later on the owner's balance. A
refusal prints its error text, because `attempt to index nil with 'Name'` names
the argument the reader must pass (that is how `FNCConvertRundownField` was
pinned); an empty table is reported as **an answer**, not a failure; and 0
order-named instances is printed as "the board is elsewhere from here", never as
"the game has no orders" (rule 7).

New `tools/1000030.py` (36 checks) runs the **real** leg — sliced out of
the file, with the build's own `spudNameKey` — against three fake worlds: one
with orders, one with nothing order-named, and one whose getter **takes money**.
Every fixture instance carries a counting `FireServer` / `InvokeServer` /
`PivotTo`, so the read-only claim is an asserted ledger (`fired=0`, `keys=0`,
`invoked=[FNCGetOrderProgress, FNCGetAvailableBoxes]`) rather than a grep, and
eight source ties pin that the allow-list literally excludes `FNCAcceptOrder` and
`EVTRefreshOrders` and that the walk recurses whether or not a node matched.
Writing it caught a real reporting defect on the first run: the world walk printed
`Company.Orders.Order1` because it rooted at `""`, so paths did not match what
`GetFullName()` would say — the root string is now `"Workspace"` and the harness
check that failed is what pinned it.

Compile OK, suite **37/37** (34 harnesses + 3 gates), headroom **5** (unchanged —
the leg is another branch of the existing **global** `spudTestWork`, so its ~15
new names are locals of that function's own prototype and the build closure never
sees them), name audit clean **380** (no new top-level name). **Harness green,
and the build is IN GAME** — the owner's **Copy report** header reads
`rebuild v1.138`, and **Run all** ran the two read-only probes on it (their
answers are the v1.138 remote re-read in §3.12, plus a `fieldremotes` leg that
again carried `(tool none)` because `Objects.Equipment` holds no implement on
this plot right now). What has **not** happened is the one press this entry
exists for: **Tests → Company orders** has its own button precisely because it
breaks the "Run all stays read-only" rule, so the four answers §6 asks are still
open. Until it is pressed once, the auto-contract reader stays unbuilt on
purpose — the alternative is a feature whose order-shape, reward field and grow
time are all invented.

**"boy do it yourself" — the Company-orders button pressed, and two lying log
lines it exposed (v1.139).** The owner's go-ahead to drive MuMu myself rather than
ask for pastes. Reaching the button needed one honest detour: the client was still
on a build whose Tests tab had no **Company orders**, and the only way to get the
new one loaded was 📤 Unload (which kills the live loops and bumps the shared
token) and a re-EXECUTE of the saved **"faf"** script — **one** build, session 3,
never a second EXECUTE beside a live one (§3.16). Then Tests (tab rail y≈660) →
**Company orders** (y≈801), and `adb -s 127.0.0.1:16416 logcat -d | grep -F
"[spud] test: orders"` carried the whole report. What §6 asked, answered:

- **(c) the doors answer with no arguments.** `FNCGetOrderProgress()` and
  `FNCGetAvailableBoxes()` both invoked clean from the field, and the balance
  bracket proved a **reader**: `coins 923235 → 923235`. One returned an **empty
  table**, the other a table holding a numeric key with value **1232** — so the
  shape is `{ [<id>] = <number> }`, and an empty answer is a real answer ("no
  order is open from here"), not a failure. `FNCAcceptOrder` and `EVTFulfillOrder`
  remain **unfired** — the allow-list worked exactly as designed.
- **(d) a grow time IS client-readable.** `CropGrowthConfigClient` answers per
  crop: **`Tomato = 2178`**, `Wheat = 45`, `Rice = 480`, `Soybean = 2178`,
  `Grape = 2400`, `Balsam = 1500`, `Cypress = 2280`, `Holly = 1800`,
  `Mint = 1800`, `Alien Tentacle = 3660`, `Steve = 600`, `Stunted Hallowood =
  1200`, plus a `Configs` table with `RSVPEvents` / `Stories`. So the owner's
  ranking rule (*"the easyest one by seeding time and cost"*) has a **time** half
  after all — the v1.123/v1.124 fear (`NO client-side grow time is readable …`)
  is closed. The **cost** half is still only what a shop trip learned:
  `no shelf price read yet this build (a seed trip names them)`.
- **(a)/(b) the order instance is still NOT in the world.** `0 instance(s) of
  20004 walked are named like an order (CAP 20000 REACHED — this is not the whole
  map)`, and the leg says the right thing about that: *nothing in the WORLD is
  named like an order — that is not "no orders", it means the board is elsewhere*.
  The `PlayerGui` walk came back with 386 label hits and **none** of them says an
  order (all Herding Mode / PalletStoreMenu / notification text), so the board is
  not merely off-screen — it is **inside a menu that has to be opened**, and
  opening it is a UI action this probe deliberately does not take. The 20
  `Modules.Larry` order/task/goal/box doors were captured **with their classes**,
  which is the other thing that was previously only a name.

**So the reader is still not writable, and the reason is narrower than before:**
the getters and the grow-time table are proven, but nothing has ever named the
instance an order lives in, and `FNCGetAvailableBoxes` answered `{}` from the
field. Rule 4 says the next step is one more press **in town / with the company
board actually on screen**, not a reader built on the assumption that
`{ [id] = 1232 }` is the whole payload.

**The two bugs the same buffer gave me, both fixed here.** Neither is a crash;
both are log lines that assert something untrue, which is the class this repo
treats as a bug (rule 7 and v1.134's status-line fix are the precedents):

1. **`⚠ still full` about a tank that had been emptied and paid.** The owner's
   v1.138 console: `no unload at DropOffs.Deposit_Warehouse after 16 s (load 0,
   coins 644584 → 923235) — the pad didn't take it while parked`, then `⚠ still
   full`, then a 60 s back-off — while the tank read **0** and the balance had
   risen ~278k. **Root cause, read from the source, no new probe needed:** the
   park trip's only proof is `cur < cap0`, and the in-place `EVTSellAtStore`
   above it is watched for just **1.5 s**, so a sale that lands a beat later
   empties the tank *while the trip is still polling* — and with `cap0 == 0` the
   drop is arithmetically unprovable. `sellHarvester` then printed its final line
   without ever re-reading. Now a park that finds the tank at 0 returns true with
   `the tank already reads empty here — the load is gone (coins … → …)`, and the
   end-of-trip verdict **re-reads** `harvesterLoad`: an empty tank is
   `✅ the tank reads empty after the pad trip`, a loaded one names the real
   number, and an unreadable one says `UNREADABLE` instead of "full".
2. **`milk store: (no trailer or silo found)` twice a minute with milk off.** The
   dropdown's own `Callback` is what logs a pick, and the store re-scan (every
   15 s) **sets** the value — Fluent's `SetValue` re-enters that Callback — so the
   script was reporting its own refresh as though the owner had chosen it, in
   every console, forever. A pick that is already the pick now returns before it
   assigns or logs. Costs no register (one line inside an existing closure).
   Also fixed in passing: `drop-off 1/1: … at (-352, -89)` printed only X and Z,
   hiding the **Y** that is the entire subject of §3.10's height story — the line
   now names all three.

**Deliberately NOT changed:** `remote harvest: fired 0 of 287 cell(s) from 47
stop(s), 3x3 per stop — 0 took, 287 left`. `#stops` in that line is the *planned*
count, so it is consistent with the grid correctly ending itself after three
blade-miss stops (v1.132's rule), and the logcat buffer no longer holds the lines
around that run — so per rule 4 the code stays as it is until a live pass says
otherwise.

Compile OK, suite **37/37**, headroom **5** (unchanged — no new top-level name;
the guard and the re-read add no build-closure local), name audit clean **380**.
`tools/1000006.py` gained **9 checks** that run the **real** `trySellAt` and
`sellHarvester` on a virtual clock (an already-empty park is a success with
**zero** presses and **zero** sell calls, the line names the empty read as the
proof, and nothing calls that pad a failure; a trip that leaves the tank at 50 is
still `⚠ … still reads 50` and never claims an empty tank), and
`tools/1000026.py` gained **2 ties** (the Callback bails before it
assigns and logs, and the 15 s scan still exists). **In-game verified:** the
Company-orders report above came from the live v1.138 client. **Not verified in
game:** these three log-line fixes — they need the next full tank and the next
milk run.

**"why does it onyl do half the field" — the block grid was cutting L-shapes
(v1.140).** The owner's console had the shape of it in one ratio, on a field the
grid had already been fired at in full:

```
remote plow: fired 361 of 361 cell(s) from 57 stop(s), 3x3 per stop — 180 took, 181 left
remote cultivate: fired 368 of 368 cell(s) from … stop(s), 3x3 per stop — 165 took, 203 left
```

361 cells over 57 stops is **6.3 cells a stop, where a 3×3 is 9**, and roughly
half of every burst was refused. **Root cause, read from the source — and it is
not the blade, the tow or the band:** `spudRadialFire` grouped the remaining
cells into rows and then took the `side`-th **row** of that list and the
`side`-th **column of each row's own list**, and parked at the **mean** of
whatever cells landed in the chunk. On a *fully bare or fully worked* field every
row holds the same columns, so a chunk is a square and the mean is its centre —
which is exactly why 32 harness fixtures never showed this. On a **part-worked**
field each row holds a different set, so the chunk comes out an **L**, its mean
sits well outside the band from its own corners, and every call to the far arm of
the L is refused. And there was **no miss line at all** — the off-block warning
and the "did the blade land" test both measure the attachment against *that same
mean*, so an L-shaped stop reads as perfectly landed. Half the field is then left
for `spudHopCells`, which puts the blade on each cell one teleport at a time, so
the field does finish — slowly, and that is what "only half" looks like from
outside.

**The fix is to cut by DISTANCE, in the game's own units.** New global
`spudFieldPitch = 10` — the spacing `cellsAtStage` itself uses
(`((x − (cols+1)/2) × 10, (y − (rows+1)/2) × 10)`) — and
`span = (side − 1) × spudFieldPitch` (= 20 at this build's 3×3) is the widest a
block may be: a band ends when the next row is further than `span` from the
band's **first** row, a block ends when the next column is further than `span`
from the group's **first** column, both still capped at `side` rows and `side`
columns, and the stop is the block's **bounding-box centre** rather than a
centroid. Three details are the whole bug class: compare against the **first**
element, not the previous one (three *counted* columns can span four pitches
across a hole, and then the corner is outside the band the side was sized
against); collect the columns the **band actually holds** instead of slicing each
row's own list (a short row must not shift the cut for every other row); and park
on the **box centre**, so a block one cell short of a square still parks where a
full square of those cells would have. The consequence is the invariant the
square rule was always claiming: the worst cell of any block is
`(span/2)·√2 = 14.1` studs out, inside the **20** the lift probe measured as
taken — so `spudBlockSide`'s corner arithmetic is now true **per block**, not
merely per shape. **The owner's equal-square rule is untouched**: `side` still
comes from `spudBlockSide(spudFieldTaken)` = 3, the caps are still `side` rows by
`side` columns, and a block can no longer grow a tail than before — the change
moves cells between blocks, it does not enlarge one.

`tools/1000016.py` gained two fixtures whose cells are **not** a
rectangle — an 8-cell **ragged** patch (middle columns missing in two of its
three rows) and two 3-cell strips **40 studs apart** — each expecting 2 stops,
every cell called **once**, every cell taken, no `leaving this block` line and
nothing left standing, on a fixture whose blade lands exactly on the commanded
stop so stop-vs-cell geometry is the only variable. They were **verified against
the pre-fix build** to prove they are the bug and not decoration: there the apart
field makes **12 calls for 6 cells from 1 stop and takes 0**, and the ragged one
takes 5 of 8. Four source ties keep the old cut out
(`(idx - 1) * side + 1`, `sx / #block`, `sz / #block`, `math.ceil(ncols / side)`
must all stay absent, and `spudRadialFire` must read `spudFieldPitch`).

Compile OK, suite **37/37**, headroom **5** (unchanged — `spudFieldPitch` is a
global assignment, and the grouping reuses the locals the old cut declared), name
audit clean **381**. **Harness green — NOT reloaded in game**, and the verdict is
the ratio the report was about: on a part-worked field
`fired N of N cell(s) from K stop(s), 3x3 per stop` should read **N/K close to
9** with `took` climbing towards `fired`, instead of 6.3 and half. If a `the
blade is N studs off stop` line now appears on a ragged field it is the honest
kind (the blade really missed), not the silence of a mis-centred block.

**The seed shop pressed a control that read `Delete Farm` (v1.141).** This one
came out of the standing directive — drive MuMu myself and keep the build
bug-free while the owner is away — not from a report. The buffer had the click
and its answer next to each other:

```
seed shop: clicking Players.ok_simba3.PlayerGui.GuiViews.HUD.Notifications.ConfirmAction.Content.ConfirmButton ('Delete Farm') — the Purchase button
seed shop: the menu is still showing after 267 visible control(s) — nothing bought.
```

**Root cause, read from the source, and it is a name/label confusion — the same
family as v1.135's `Tempo L16`, run backwards.** `guiTextOf(btn)` returns the
control's `Text`, else the first non-empty `TextLabel` descendant, else
**`btn.Name`**; `harvestMenu` then put `label .. " " .. g.Name:lower()` into one
`text` field and the picker matched buy words against that join. So `confirm`
arrived from the **instance name** `ConfirmButton` and outranked the label the
player actually reads — `Delete Farm` — and the old `forbidden` list
(`robux`, `premium`, `gems`, `close`) held **no destructive word at all**. When
nothing matched, the **third tier clicked the first usable control**, which on
that screen meant picking one of 267. Three things were wrong and all three are
now rules (§3.13): a **danger** word in *either* half refuses the click (the new
list adds `sell`, `cancel`, `back`, `exit`, `delete`, `destroy`, `remove`,
`reset`, `unsubscribe`, `leave`); a **buy** word counts in the **label**, and the
name may speak only when the label cannot — i.e. when `label == name`, the
image-only Purchase control v1.113 proved this game really draws, which is why
the fallback exists and why throwing it away would break the working buy; and
**there is no third tier**. A buy-shaped control that is also dangerous is not
swallowed, it is named (`a buy-shaped control was REFUSED, not clicked — …`), and
when nothing on screen reads as a buy the run prints the visible labels and
`clicking nothing` — the honest dead end the repo's own rule 7 asks for, instead
of a second destructive press. `harvestMenu` keeps `label` and `name` as separate
fields now, and the **Shop counter** probe was fixed with the same two
predicates, because a probe that clicks what the farm refuses to click is worse
than no probe. `shownPurchase()` deliberately still reads the joined text: it
only *locates* the dialog's container so the close step can shut it, and never
clicks what it finds (rule 16).

New `tools/1000034.py` (36 checks + 9 ties) runs the **real** tier block —
sliced out of `buySeedAndFill` and wrapped so its `break` is legal — against
fixtures that carry a label and a name apart. The ones that carry weight: the
incident itself (`spudShopBuyChoice("Delete Farm", "ConfirmButton")` is **false**
and `spudShopDanger` returns `"delete"`, and the block clicks neither); a control
whose name says `deletefarmbutton` but whose label says `confirm` is **refused
and logged**; `("Purchase", "CancelPurchase")` is refused on the name alone; the
real buys still get clicked, `why` and all (`purchase` → `the Purchase button`,
plus `buy now`, `confirm`, `checkout`); the image-only case still works
(`purchasebutton`/`purchasebutton`, `("", "buyseeds")`) while `("", "")` and
`(nil, nil)` are never a buy; `buy with robux` is buy-shaped **and** refused; the
seed tile tier still earns its click (`why == "the Wheat tile"`) but a
`cancelbutton` tile is refused; and with two candidates — a refused one in front
of a safe Purchase — the safe one wins rather than the first. The ties refuse the
build if `isPurchase`, `forbidden(` or the `first usable control` tier comes
back, if either predicate is handed `b.text` instead of `(b.label, b.name)`, or if
`harvestMenu` stops recording the two halves separately.

Compile OK, suite **38/38** (35 harnesses + 3 gates), headroom **5** (unchanged —

assignments, the split record is inside `harvestMenu`'s own prototype, and the
tier rewrite reuses `target`/`why`/`refused`), name audit clean **384**. **Harness
green — NOT reloaded in game.** Same buffer, four things noticed and deliberately
**not** changed (rule 4: they need evidence, not my guess): the seed pass was
buying **Rice** with no `seed default: Tomato` line anywhere (v1.135's verdict
still open), `remote seed: fired 9 of 2105 … 0 took` is the **pre-v1.140** grid on

`the seed crate didn't come up on attempt 1 (hands still empty) — the game offers: Carry`

**The milk loop empties a pile, but one buried box can cost a whole pass
(v1.142).** The owner's standing ask was *"finnishe the milk loop"*, and the
store deposit was the half that looked broken: a session buffer full of

```
store: 118 milk jug(s) waiting — destination LH150 at 8/180
store: E did not pick up Workspace.Objects.DraggableObjects.MilkContainer — the game offers: nothing visible
store: nothing in hand after the double grab — retrying next pass
store: store: the milk jugs could not be reached
```

Read on a **timestamped** buffer rather than the 60-line tail (the whole
21:58:29–22:09:43 window, on the shipped v1.141 client), it is not a lockout —
it is one box that refuses while the rest of the pile waits behind it:

- The refusals and the successes **interleave on the same build**: `3 jug(s)
  into LH150 in 53.3s (23/180)` at 22:01:08, then `9 … (33/180)`, `13 …
  (46/180)`, `15 … (61/180)`, `12 … (83/180)`, `13 … (112/180)` and
  `8 jug(s) … (126/180)` — **72** `carrying MilkContainer` lines against
  **144** refusals, and only **5** of the ~12 passes ended `could not be
  reached`. The pile went 118 → `0 left outside` and the pass then correctly
  reported `no milk jugs waiting at this plot`.
- **The stuck passes are the loop, not the game.** The jug list is sorted by
  distance from the *character* and a failed grab changes nothing about it, so
  `jugs[1]` was re-picked on every one of the twenty iterations. One refusal
  costs ~2.7 s (0.5 remote + 0.6 carry-up + 0.9 E + 0.7 button, all bounded
  waits, on an 11 fps client), so a pass that hammers one box spends its whole
  budget there and leaves 117 boxes behind it — which is exactly the difference
  between `1 jug in 54.3 s` and `13 jugs in 48.5 s`.

Two changes, and no third (rule 16 — the mechanism that refuses a jug is still
not measured, so it is instrumented, not guessed). **One attempt per jug**: a
`tried` set keyed on the model, so a pass surveys different boxes instead of
re-teleporting at one, and the pass that used to end at 1 jug now spends its
iterations on the rest of the pile. And the failure line **becomes the probe**
(global `milkJugMissText`, §3.4), because every jug at a plot is named
`MilkContainer` and `GetFullName()` cannot tell one from another:

```
store: E did not pick up jug MilkContainer at (-412, 135, 59), I am (-417, 162, 54)
57.3 studs off (5.8 flat) — 33 jug(s) stacked on it, owner no tag, on foot, tp ran,
offers: nothing visible | registry: Carry(hidden) · FillLiquidCarrier(visible)
```

Each field separates a hypothesis the old line could not: **`tp ran` vs `tp
REFUSED to move`** (the teleport not landing — `milkTp`'s return value was
thrown away before this build), **`N jug(s) stacked on it`** (a trigger that can
only hold one claimant, counted within 4 studs on all three axes), **`owner …`**
read from the jug's **own** `Configurations`/attribute and never from a parent —
`milkStoreOwnerOf` walks eight levels up, and a folder that says so is not an
answer about this box — with no tag printed as `no tag`, never as `mine`
(rule 7), **`on foot` vs `seated in X`** (a seated client presses nothing), and
**`offers:` next to `registry:`** — the visible billboards versus every button
the game's `PCGP_ContextActions.Buttons` holds, with the hidden ones **marked**,
because a `Carry` that exists but is not *shown* is the picker refusing this
jug, while a registry with no `Carry` at all is a different bug. The rich line
prints for the first three jugs of a pass; after that the registry has already
been read out, so a 20-jug pass cannot fill the console with the same dump. The
pass ends `store: the milk jugs could not be reached — N different jug(s) tried
this pass`, and the count is the diagnosis: `1` is a single buried box, `20` is
the whole pile refusing this client.

Compile OK, suite **38/38** (35 harnesses + 3 gates — no new harness: the jug

**"when it puts seeds back in the seeder it isnt filling it up to 600 only about 100"
— three defects, one build (v1.143).** The owner's ask is the middle one; the other
two came out of the same MuMu buffer while working on it, and all three are root-caused
from the console plus the source, not from a new probe.

**(1) The seed trip stopped at whatever the tank could hold in four visits — because
the visit budget was planned on a cap the pass could not read.** This is the owner's
report, and the live arithmetic names the mechanism:

```
no seeder the game calls a Planter is readable (TempoL16=Planter (stored) · …) — seed state unknown
seeder EMPTY with 2358 cell(s) … refill 2/6: buying Rice and filling
the seeder is still empty and this run already made 4 shop trip(s) (the cap is 4) — not teleporting again (pick a different seed, or fill it by hand)
✅ seeder now holds 50/600 seeds (Rice)
```

Two sites plan the same trip budget and only one of them could see the machine.
`seedPasses` reads the seeder for its `refill N/M` promise and got **6**;
`buySeedAndFill`'s entry gate re-read it a moment later, the seeder was stored (or
otherwise unreadable), and `spudSeedTripsNeeded(nil, …)` correctly answers **nil** —
rule 7, an unknown capacity is unknown, not one trip — so `spudSeedTripCeiling`
did what it only ever does and **fell back to its floor, 4**. The pass then promised
six visits and was refused at the fourth, so the 600-slot tank got what four trips
could pour (the same buffer reads `50/600` on one trip and `58/600` on the next —
a crate that poured 50 and a partial second box). The fix is not a bigger constant;
it is that **a cap this build has already read stands in for a cap it cannot read
now**. New globals `spudSeederCap` (written by `seederSeedState` whenever a real
`MaxCapacity > 0` arrives) and `spudSeederCapFor(cap, remembered)` (fresh number
wins, else the remembered one tagged `"remembered"`, else nil — so the floor still
applies to a build that has literally never seen the tank). Both plan sites read it,
and the refusal line **says which it used**: `planned on a remembered tank of 600`
rather than letting a failed property read silently shrink the promise the same pass
just made. The owner's `100` is this bug's visible half; the trip budget now scales
to the tank on both sides of the promise.

**(2) The trip clicked the pallet till, and the `break` is why.** The v1.141 rule set
(refuse danger words, read the label not the joined text, no arbitrary-click tier) was
right and still bought nothing, because the tier loop **took the first safe buy-shaped
control it found** — and on this screen that is not the seed dialog:

```
seed shop: clicking Players.ok_simba3.PlayerGui.GuiViews.Stores.PalletStoreMenu.Main.Border.Content.InfoPanel.PurchaseButton ('Buy') — the Purchase button
seed shop: the menu is still showing after 246 visible control(s) — nothing bought.
⚠ the Rice purchase could NOT be confirmed (the balance didn't move)
```

That is the **pallet** till — its label genuinely says `Buy`, so it passes every word
rule, and §5's v1.107 entry already recorded what it answers: *"You need to own the
Forklift License!"* The three-click repeat and the unmoved balance are the same incident
read again from the emulator's logcat today (that buffer window held no `rebuild v…`
line, so it names the console's words, not the build that printed them). The path tells the two
screens apart, so the trip now collects **every** safe buy-shaped control with its full
lowercased path and hands the list to the new global `spudShopBuyPick`, which passes over
anything under a `pallet` or `forklift` name in favour of a control that is not — so the
real Purchase still gets clicked from behind the till. Only those two words refuse,
because both came off that live path and that toast; an invented third word would refuse
the real buy (rule 4). A screen holding **only** that till is now a dead end said out
loud (`every buy-shaped control on screen is the pallet till (…) — it sells pallets, not
seed — clicking nothing for seed`) instead of a licence the script can buy.

**(3) The crate never came to hand, and the fill reported itself done anyway.** Every
`carryCrate` call site teleports the character **onto** the crate, and §5's v1.108
entry is the game fact that makes that fail: a hop onto a draggable throws the client off
it, so the offer the build reads is never the box's `Carry` prompt. The refusals read from
the emulator are `EnterEquipment` (a hop onto a neighbouring ride) and `nothing` (the box
not offering from that pose at all) — the full three-attempt chain, and what the caller did
with it:

```
seed crate: the bought crate didn't come up on attempt 1 (hands still empty) — the game offers: nothing
seed crate: the bought crate didn't come up on attempt 2 (hands still empty) — the game offers: nothing
seed crate: the bought crate didn't come up on attempt 3 (hands still empty) — the game offers: nothing
seed crate: the crate didn't come to hand — nothing was poured (…)
✅ the seeder holds 3 seed(s) now
```

Two changes, both inside the trip. `carryCrate` now **stands beside** the box
(`spudCarryNear = 8` studs out along the character's own approach, three 50 ms frames for
the pose to reach the server — rule 11) before the E attempts, and it names the offer set
verbatim when the read does not contain `Carry` through the new global
`spudOfferSaysCarry` — which **fails open**: an unreadable offer list costs the press, not
the attempt (rule 7). And the leg that used to `return true` after
`the crate didn't come to hand` — the lie that made a failed grab read as a fill, spent
four shop trips on it and then reported the pass complete — returns **false**, so
`buySeedAndFill` stops with the reason in the line above it rather than pouring an empty
hand. `tools/1000010.py` pins the fail-open ordering (the step-aside sits
before the attempt loop), the verbatim-offer rule, and refuses the build if either
`didn't come to hand` exit hands back a bare `return true`.

it passed — the compile row's bug, in a new row (§2.2); the label is lowercase now.
**Harness green — NOT reloaded in game**, and the three verdicts are the lines above
read on the next seed trip: `planned on a remembered tank of 600` with a budget that
reaches the pass's own `refill N/M` and a tank that climbs past 100; the real dialog
clicked with the balance **falling**, or the honest `clicking nothing for seed`; and no

seed(s)`. If the till note appears on a screen that genuinely has no seed till, the
answer is the same as §6 item 7's: the buy happens elsewhere and the next evidence is a
**Shop counter** probe read, not a third word added to the refuse list.

**"only about 100", read from the owner's own v1.142 console — the trip delivers
one crate while the plan budgets for two (v1.144).** The owner pasted the whole
SPUD CONSOLE rather than a symptom, and it answered the v1.143 question with a
number this build had never printed: `refill 2/6` beside
`✅ seeder now holds 50/600 seeds (Potato)`. The remembered-cap fix is working
(the plan says 6, not 4), so the tank still does not fill for a **different**
reason, and the console names it three lines earlier:

```
… still working (shop trip: carrying a second Potato crate)
seed shop: ⚠ Stores is still open after handler then again — close it by hand once
seed shop: ⚠ the second crate's dialog (Stores) is still up — close it by hand once
seed crate: the second purchase did not land — putting the crates back down and
           picking up only the one that is paid for
✅ seeder now holds 50/600 seeds (Potato) — the planting pass takes it from here
```

**Root cause 1 — the plan divides by a request, not a delivery.** A 600-slot
tank and a 50-seed crate is **twelve** crates. `spudCratesPerTrip = 2` is what
the trip *asks* the till for; the console shows the ask fails on this client
every time, so six visits carry six crates = 300 seeds and the pass stops with
the tank still reading empty. Nothing in the build ever recorded how many
crates a trip actually came back with. **Fix:** a confirmed buy now ends with
`spudCratesTripGot = 1 + extraCrates`, and every budget (the entry gate, the
refill ceiling, the short-pour line) divides by the new global
`spudCratesTripActual()` — the learned count, clamped to the ask, the constant
until a trip has finished. A one-crate till is planned at **12** visits and the
refusal prints the whole arithmetic
(`the cap is 12, planned on a read tank of 600 at 1 crate(s) a trip`).
`tools/1000033.py` runs it: `a one-crate till needs TWELVE visits, not
six` and `the ceiling raises the floor to that` are the owner's complaint as an
assertion.

**Root cause 2 — the dialog judge could not tell the seed popup from the till.**
`shownPurchase()` answers *"did the buy popup shut?"* and returned the **first**
purchase-shaped control in all of `GuiViews`. This town keeps
`Stores.PalletStoreMenu…PurchaseButton` on screen (§5 v1.107), so a dialog that
had closed still read `Stores is still open after handler then again`, the close
step chased the **till's** container, and the second crate was bought against a
till that was never shut — which is the mechanism behind root cause 1's failed
ask. v1.143 fixed the same confusion for the *click* and left the *judge*
reading the old way. **Fix:** the judge now collects every purchase-shaped
control with its full path and hands the list to the same
`spudShopBuyPick`, so one rule covers the click, the Shop-counter probe and the
close verdict; a till-only screen means nothing to close, not a phantom dialog.

**Root cause 3 — five retries clicked the same wrong card.** The same paste ran
the store failure twice, and the diagnostic it prints answered itself:

```
equipment manager: looking for 'TempoL16' — 6 card(s) showing right now:
  Tempo L16 · JD Shank · Horsch Joker · JD S790 · Tempo L16 · Challenger MT9
equipment manager: called the game's own Store handler for TempoL16 (Activated…)
store: retrying the TempoL16 (2/5)… … (5/5)…
couldn't store the TempoL16 after 5 tries — giving up on it and moving to the next
```

**Two cards named `Tempo L16`** — this farm owns two of the planter. `findCard`
returned the first match, `storeOne` retried five times against that same card,
and the tool stayed out (the run later hitched it 33 studs away, so the proof
was honest: the store never happened). A name is not an identity. **Fix:**
`findCard` gathers **every** card carrying the folded name and answers the
`which`-th (wrapping); `managerAction(vehicleName, action, which)` takes the
number and `storeOne` passes its `attempt`; the final refusal names what it
still holds (`still in the world` / `GONE`, its own `InStorage`, plus
`spudEquipHereText()`), because **which** twin is the out machine is not
measured yet (rule 4) — the next console says it.

**What the paste also proves, and what is deliberately untouched.** The
v1.143/v1.142 carry fix is confirmed working on the live client —
`the seed crate didn't come up on attempt 1 (hands still empty) — the game
offers: Carry` followed by `✅ carrying the seed crate (E, attempt 2)` — so the
grab is a first-attempt miss, not the v1.143 dead-hand case. The tow is clean
(`hitch gap at arrival: 0.9 studs`, `0.4`), `lined up HitchRoot ↔ Hitch (gap
2.28 studs)` is v1.135's socket measurement landing, and the grid took 45 of
its 50 seeds with the seeder's own budget cutting it short — correct behaviour,
not the v1.140 defect. The `seed shop: buy popup — Title | … | TextLabel`
hundred-label dump is noisy and stays (rule 16) until a live buy is misjudged
by it. The `1 crate(s) a trip` line will only appear after a trip has actually
finished, and the store cycle only helps if the two cards really differ — both
verdicts are the next console's, not mine.

`the second purchase did not land` moved `1000033.py`'s window 11,000

`Stores is still open after handler then again` on a screen whose only other

**"it's still only putting 100 seeds in" — one `FNCFillSeeder` is every crate
in hand (v1.145).** The owner reported the symptom a second time, and this time
the live buffer had the number that v1.144's fix could not explain: the till
**did** sell both crates (`✅ second Potato crate bought via
FNCRequestStorePurchase (balance 1375937 → 1255937) — the seeder gets both from
this one trip`), the trip **did** deliver two crates, and the seeder still read
`101/600`. Reading the fill leg line by line closed it:

```
seeder fill: FNCFillSeeder(seeder) — the seeder had 1 seeds
seeder fill: the hands are empty and the tank still wants 499 seed(s) of 600 —
             3 more town visit(s) at 2 crate(s) a trip
✅ seeder now holds 101/600 seeds (Potato) — the planting pass takes it from here
```

**Root cause: the crate size was learned from a press, not from a crate.** There
is no `round N poured …` line between the fill and the verdict because there was
no second round — `FNCFillSeeder` drains **every crate in hand in one call**, so
the rise the pour loop records as `spudSeedsPerCrate` was **100** (two crates),
the crate is **50**, and the trip arithmetic then halves:
`spudSeedTripsNeeded(600, 101, 100, 2)` is **3**, and 3 visits × 2 crates × 50
seeds is 300 of the 499 the tank still wants. v1.144's `spudCratesTripActual()`
was correct and could not help, because the divisor that mattered was the other
one. The same bug was in the round loop's own learning (`gave2 = n1 - before`).

**Fix:** both sites read `spudCratesInHand()` on both sides of the call and
divide the rise by the crates that call actually consumed — floored, still only
ever raised (so a half-poured box cannot teach a size smaller than a full one),
and falling back to 1 when the hands cannot be read (rule 7: unknown, never a
guess of 2). The round line now names both numbers
(`round N poured 100 more from 2 crate(s) (50 a crate)`) and the short-pour line
prints the size it planned on, so the next console shows the arithmetic instead
of requiring this post-mortem.

`tools/1000033.py` runs the contrast on the pure arithmetic:
`the same shortfall with a 50-seed crate needs FIVE visits` (5) versus
`the two-crate rise read as one crate is the defect` (3), plus that 5 × 2 × 50
covers the tank and 3 × 2 × 50 does not. Its crate-size tie was itself the old
defect written down — it asserted `spudSeedsPerCrate = gave` — so it now refuses
that form and requires the divided one, plus a tie that both pour sites count
the crates they consumed.

Compile OK, suite **38/38**, headroom **5** (unchanged — all eight new names are

**"when they hitch is there a way to force them to hitch together" — the weld
is measured shut, so this build makes the miss readable (v1.146).** The question
has a direct answer from the record, and the live buffer said which half of it
is still open.

**Already forced, and it works when the game exposes the join.** The line-up
measures the ride's `Components.Hitchable` against the tool's
`Chassis.HitchableSocket` and parks them together before it presses anything,
and the join itself is the game's own door (`FNCAttemptHitch`, X as the
fallback). Tonight's console: `lined up HitchRoot ↔ Hitch (gap 5.94 studs)` →
`attempt 1: hitch prompt shows — pressing X` → `✅ hitched TempoL16 (attempt 1)`
→ `✅ tug passed (3.9 studs)`, and `hitch gap at arrival: 2.2 studs`.

**"Force them to be one rigid thing" was built and refused.** That is exactly
v1.133's client `WeldConstraint` between the two hitch parts: live it did close
the gap (`hitch gap at arrival: 1.1 studs`) and the rig answered by throwing a
wheel onto the field next to a tractor reading `Speed 0`, while the very next
grid took `0 of 98` cells on the field that had taken 1793 of 2388 without it.
The reason is Roblox's own assembly rule, not a guess: the game's hitch
constraint already joins ride and implement into **one assembly**, so a second
joint between the same pair is a loop and a loop is over-constrained. v1.134
deleted it and the source says *"Do not re-add a weld to the tow."*

**What is actually left is a socket this client cannot see.** The same buffer:

```
✅ hitched HorschJoker (attempt 1)
… still working (hitching the HorschJoker) ×5
tool HorschJoker has no hitch point (309 instance(s) read) — using creep alignment
park: hitch line-up parts missing — pressing the fallback anyway
```

309 descendants and neither `Chassis.HitchableSocket` nor a hitch-shaped
`BasePart` among them — so there is nothing to aim at, the precise park is
given up, and the creep spends the attempts. Counting instances proves the
client holds the machine; it says nothing about what the game calls its join,
and rule 4 forbids guessing a fourth attachment name. **So the miss line now
names what it did read**: up to eight instances on that model whose own name
says `hitch` / `socket` / `attach` / `connector`, each with its class and path,
or the honest `nothing on it is named like a hitch, socket or attachment`. One
run either hands the line-up its point or closes the question of whether this
implement can be aimed at all — and the walk is `pcall`-guarded and capped, so
a huge tool cannot hang the pass.

Compile OK, suite **38/38**, headroom **5** (unchanged — `near`, `nm` and `d`
are locals of the global `spudHitchMissText`'s own prototype), name audit clean

**"when it's doing the 3x3 make it wait 100ms after before going to the next
3x3" (v1.147).** A feel number from the owner, taken as stated. The block grid
already waits for the blade to land and takes 0.25 s after it arrives before
firing (§3.12), but it jumped to the next stop the frame its nine calls were
done — and a block's calls leave the server mid-update. The grid now waits
`spudBlockPause` ms between blocks. The constant is declared in
**milliseconds**, not seconds, because 100 is the number the owner gave and it
should be editable without translating it; `i < #stops` keeps the last stop
from paying a beat that leads nowhere. `tools/1000016.py` counts the
durations a real 2-stop grid waits, so the check is the number and its place,
not a grep: `the beat is the owner's 100 ms, not a guessed sleep`,
`a 2-stop grid pays the beat ONCE — the last stop waits for nothing`, and
`the beat does not cost the field a cell` (still 15 took). Compile OK, suite
**38/38**, headroom **5** (the one new name is a global), name audit clean
**393**. **Harness green — NOT reloaded in game**, and the verdict is the grid's
own timing: the same `fired N of N … 3x3 per stop` result, 0.1 s slower a stop.

**"again, it's not filling the seeder all the way full before starting to seed"
— fill the tank, then plant (v1.148).** The owner reported the same symptom a
third time, and this time the live buffer said plainly that the arithmetic was
no longer the problem — the **order of operations** was:

```
seeder fill: the hands are empty and the tank still wants 550 seed(s) of 600 —
             11 more town visit(s) at 1 crate(s) a trip, 50 seed(s) a crate
seed crate: the second Potato crate costs 120000 and the balance is 9026 after
            the first buy — stopping at the crate that is paid for
✅ the seeder holds 50 seed(s) now — seeding this run (no second run needed)
remote seed: fired 48 of 1898 cell(s) from 221 stop(s), 3x3 per stop — 48 took, 1850 left
still 1850 cell(s) across 1 field(s) need seed after 5 refill(s) — stopping this pass
run ended — the last stage it reached was: shop trip: the seeder is empty
```

`50 seed(s) a crate` and `11 more town visit(s)` are v1.145's fix working, and
the single-crate delivery is the **price gate doing its job** — a Potato crate is
120,000 and the balance was 9,026. So the tank could not be filled by money
that run, but it also would not have been filled by *behaviour*: two defects in
`seedCheck`, both read from the source.

**(a) Any seed counted as ready.** `if count > 0 then … going to hitch it;
return true` — a machine the previous pass left at 48/600 went straight to the
field and planted 48 cells. In the planting stage the branch now asks the
machine itself (`hungry = allowBuy and cap and cap > 0 and
spudSeederWantsMore(count, cap)`) and a hungry tank falls through to the fill.
Outside the planting stage (`allowBuy` false) it still returns ready without
spending — the read-only rule from v1.116 is untouched.

**(b) One trip, then plant.** An empty tank bought one crate and left, because
the trip is one call and the pour stops when the hands are empty. `seedCheck`
now loops the **same proven trip** while the tank reads room, counting every
iteration against the scaled ceiling the arithmetic already computes (11 for a
550-seed shortfall at one crate a trip). The owner's *"or there is not mroe
moeny"* needed no new code: the trip's own price gate refuses an unaffordable
crate **without spending** and returns false, so the loop ends on the wallet
rather than hammering a 120,000-coin till it cannot pay. The end line names
which stop it hit, because `the seeder holds 50 seed(s) now — seeding this run`
was the line that made all of this look like success.

**And the balance the log prints was not the wallet.** `seed shop: balance
129026 → 87069` sat next to `the balance is 9026 after the first buy` in the
same trip, and 129,026 − 120,000 is exactly 9,026: the proof-of-purchase breaks
on the **first drop it sees**, and the HUD's coin counter animates down, so the
captured number was a frame of the animation. The drop stays the proof; the
reported figure is now a settled re-read, because these are the numbers the
owner reads to decide whether a fill is affordable.

Compile OK, suite **38/38**, headroom **5** (unchanged — `hungry`, `settled`,
`seeder2`/`have2`/`cap2` and `seederEnd`/`heldCap` are all locals of the
function they serve), name audit clean **393** (no new global).
`tools/1000033.py` gained five ties that refuse the old shapes: a
`count > 0` return without the `hungry` question, a fill path that is not a
loop, a loop that does not count its own trips, and `after = after or
coinBalance()` as a report. **Harness green — NOT reloaded in game.** The
verdict is the trip line: `seeder fill: 50/600 — back to town for more Potato
(trip 2 of 11)`, ending either at `the tank is full, seeding this run` or at
the honest `the money or the trip budget stopped the fill — planting with what
it holds`. **Practical note the owner should decide on, not this build:** at
120,000 a crate, a full 600-slot Potato tank is **1,440,000 coins** of seed for
600 planted cells, and the balance in that buffer was 9,026 — the fill will stop
on the wallet every time until the farm is earning again.

**"STILL not filling the planter all the way nor planting the whole field" —
the trip budget was sized for one tank, not for the field (v1.150).** The owner
reported the fill a fourth time, and this time the live buffer showed the fill
**working** and the field still unfinished — so the complaint was really two
things and only one of them had been fixed. First the good half, from the same
client on v1.149:

```
seeder fill: 200/600 — back to town for more Rice (trip 3 of 6)
seeder fill: 500/600 — back to town for more Rice (trip 6 of 6)
✅ the seeder holds 600/600 seed(s) now — the tank is full, seeding this run
```

Then the half that was still broken:

```
the seeder still wants seed and this run already made 6 shop trip(s)
  (the cap is 6, planned on a remembered tank of 600 at 2 crate(s) a trip)
the refill put no seeds in the seeder — stopping this pass
Field: 0 bare, 0 plowed, 1800 cultivated, 600 seeded, 600 cuttable, 2400 total cells
run ended — the last stage it reached was: shop trip: the seeder is empty
```

**Root cause, read from the arithmetic rather than a guess:**
`spudSeedTripsNeeded(cap=600, have=0, perCrate=50, cratesPerTrip=2)` is **6** —
six visits fills the *machine* — and both budgets (the entry ceiling and
`seedPasses`' `refillsMax`) were planned on exactly that. A 600-seed tank plants
600 cells, so on a **2400-cell** field the run spends its entire trip budget
filling the first tank, every later refill is refused by the same ceiling, and
1800 cells stay Cultivated for the rest of the session while `check-back`
reports `seed:yes` forever. The number the budget has to cover is the **cells
that need seed**, not the slots in the machine: 2400 cells is 48 crates, 24
visits at two crates each.

**Fix:** the entry plan takes the larger of the two terms
(`fieldTrips = spudSeedTripsNeeded(needCells, 0, spudSeedsPerCrate,
cratesPerTrip)`), and each refill round re-sizes `refillsMax` from the count it
just re-read (`after`), so a field that shrinks as it is planted never inflates
the budget again. `spudSeedTripCeiling` still only ever raises, an unreadable
field is still **nil** (rule 7 — unknown, not one visit), and the trip's own
price gate remains the thing that stops a broke run. The refusal line now names
the field too: `planned on a remembered tank of 600 for 2400 cell(s) at 2
crate(s) a trip`.

`tools/1000033.py` runs the arithmetic both ways — `a 2400-cell field
needs TWENTY-FOUR visits at 2 crates of 50` (24) against `one tank's plan is the
defect that left 1800 cells cultivated` (6), plus `so the field number is the
one that must win` and `an unreadable field is unknown, not one visit` — and
three ties refuse the build if the entry plan drops the `needCells` term, if the
refill rounds stop re-sizing `refillsMax`, or if the refusal line loses the cell
count. Compile OK, suite **38/38**, headroom **5** (`fieldTrips` is a local of
`seedCheck`; the refill raise reuses the `refillsMax` local that already
existed), name audit clean **393** (no new global). **Harness green — NOT
reloaded in game**, and the verdict is the census line: `Field: … N cultivated,
600 seeded …` must shrink toward `0 cultivated` across the refills instead of
stopping at 1800, and the run must end `✅ seeding complete — no field is
missing seed any more`.

**"found a bug, look at logs" — an EMPTY harvester was being called full
(v1.151).** The buffer showed the whole field standing and the harvest ending
without a single call:

```
remote harvest: 600 free slot(s) cannot cover 2400 standing cell(s) — selling first, then coming back
harvester is FULL (0/600, after the remote pass) — unloading
harvester FULL (0/600) — going to Fulfillment
fulfillment pad: DropOffs.Deposit_Warehouse at (-352, 139, -89)
DropOffs.Deposit_Warehouse: the tank already reads empty here — the load is gone, coins 1664376 → 1664376
arc back to Field to finish the harvest
2400 cell(s) never took a harvest call even with the blade on them — leaving them for the next cycle
run ended — the last stage it reached was: harvest pass
```

**Root cause, read from the predicate rather than a guess:** v1.86's rule is
`spudHarvestFull(cur, max, need) = cur >= max or (max − cur) < need`, written
for `49/50 against 46 standing cells` — one free slot could never cover the
field, so firing one refused call and then refusing to sell was a lock. On a
600-slot JDS790 facing a **2400-cell** field, `free < need` is true *whatever*
the load, including at `cur = 0`. So an empty machine was declared full, sent to
sell nothing (v1.139's already-empty fix reported the truth — `the tank already
reads empty here`, coins unmoved), came back, `spudFieldRemotePass` refused to
fire for the same reason, and the pass ended with every cell standing. The
`🌱 early cut: 2400 cell(s) still read cuttable after 4 restart(s) that took
nothing` line is `spudCutWatch` doing its job — the spin guard proved the loop
was locked rather than slow, and it is why the run waited like a normal cycle
instead of restarting on a dime.

**Fix — one clause:** the "space cannot cover" branch now requires `cur > 0`.
A field bigger than one tank is the normal case, handled by filling 600,
selling, and coming back for the next 600; the rule only bites when there is a
real load whose remaining space cannot cover what is standing, which is exactly
the v1.86 case and stays.

`tools/1000006.py` already had `space: an empty tank never unloads` —
`spudHarvestFull(0, 50, 46) == false` — **and it passed for the wrong reason**:
46 cells fit inside a 50-slot tank, so `free < need` was false regardless of the
load. That is this repo's oldest lesson again (a green assertion can be the
defect), so the new checks are the case the field actually has:
`an empty tank facing a field BIGGER than it is not full` (0/600 vs 2400),
`the same field with a real load still unloads first` (599/600), and
`a partial load whose space cannot cover the field still unloads` (150/600) —
the last one written down so the v1.86 protection is not lost with the fix.

Compile OK, suite **38/38**, headroom **5** (no new name), name audit clean
**393**. **Harness green — NOT reloaded in game**, and the verdict is that the
harvest fires at all: `remote harvest: fired N of N … N took` with the census
`Harvestable` falling across the cycle instead of
`2400 cell(s) never took a harvest call even with the blade on them`. What this
build does NOT change is the harvest's own reach question — the earlier
`none is within 44 studs of the blade (24 measured + the JDS790's own width)`
line is the corner pass correctly handing the field to the blocks, and the
blocks are now reached.

**"another bug" — the same rule sold a two-unit load across town (v1.152).** The
v1.151 fix moved the lock by one step rather than out of it, and the next
logcat window said so in four lines:

```
remote harvest: fired 600 of 2400 cell(s) from 280 stop(s), 3x3 per stop — 599 took, 1801 left
harvester is FULL (599/600, after the single-spot fire) — unloading
…
remote harvest: 598 free slot(s) cannot cover 1798 standing cell(s) — selling first, then coming back
harvester is FULL (2/600) — going to Fulfillment
```

So the first round worked exactly as designed — 600 called, 599 taken, sold at
the pad for 20,594 coins (`coins 1673802 → 1694396`), back to the field. Then the
second round read the tank at **2** units
(the arc back onto the plate stages a cell or two by itself: the census went
`1800 … still standing` → `1798 cells need it`), `598 free < 1798 standing` was
true, and the run hopped **1,005 studs** to the warehouse and **1,029 back** to
pay **71 coins** for those two cells (`coins 1699492 → 1699563`) — the second
sale of an almost-empty tank in this one cycle, after v1.151's window caught the
first at `0/600`. With the room left always short of 1798, the arithmetic says
every round does the same, so 1798 ripe crops were never going to be cut. Nothing
errored and no line lied — the census simply crept (`1800 … still standing` →
`1798 cells need it`), which is why the symptom reads as a slow farm rather than
a broken one. **Where those 2 units came from is not measured** (the pass reads
the tank before it fires anything, and the sale above reported the tank empty);
it does not change the gate, which is what the run does with a load of 2.

**Root cause, and it is the same mistake v1.151 only half closed:** the second
branch of `spudHarvestFull` compared the tank's **remaining room** against the
**whole standing field**. On a 600-slot harvester facing 2400 cells that is true
of every load, including one crate's worth, so "full" stopped meaning *the
machine is out of room* and started meaning *the field is bigger than the
machine* — which is the normal case, handled by filling, selling, and coming
back. v1.86's `49/50` is a dead end for a different reason, and the reason is the
**machine**: a block stop fires `side²` = **9** cells, so a tank with less room
than one stop can never spend the grid, whatever is standing.

**Fix:** the branch is now `max − cur < math.min(need, side * side)` with
`side = spudBlockSide(spudFieldTaken)` (both already globals, so the local is
free and rule 14's ordering holds). `2/600` against 1798 → not full, keep
harvesting; `150/600` → not full; `595/600` and `599/600` → full, unload; and
`49/50` against **46** cells → full (v1.86 kept) while `49/50` against **1**
cell → keep working and take that last tile before travelling for it. `need`
survives only as the "there IS standing work" guard and as the cap that makes the
one-cell case work.

`tools/1000006.py` now slices the **real** `spudBlockSide` and reads
`spudFieldTaken` / `spudFieldPitch` out of the source instead of hard-coding
them, pins `one block stop fires 3x3 = 9 cells on this build`, and replaces the
check that was this defect written down — v1.151's
`a partial load whose space cannot cover the field still unloads` (150/600 =
**true**) is now `a load with room for a whole stop is not a sale` (false), next
to `two units in a 600-slot tank keep harvesting — the live 2/600 lock`. Both
were **verified against the old arithmetic** before the edit shipped: the two
formulas agree on all six recorded cases (`0/600`, `595/600`, `599/600`, `49/50`
both ways, `0/50`) and differ only on `2/600 vs 1798` and `150/600 vs 2400` —
which is the whole bug and nothing else.

That makes **two** builds in a row where a green harness assertion was the
defect: the check `spudHarvestFull(0, 50, 46) == false` passed while an empty
tank was being called full, because 46 fits in 50; and the check
`spudHarvestFull(150, 600, 2400) == true` passed while a two-unit tank was being
sold across town, because it asserted the *field comparison* rather than asking
what makes a tank stuck. A new arithmetic rule needs a check that FAILS on the
old formula — which the two-rule comparison above is now written down for.

Compile OK, suite **38/38**, headroom **5** (unchanged — `side` is a local inside
the global's own body), name audit clean **393** (no new top-level name).
**Harness green — NOT reloaded in game**, and the verdict is the harvest filling
before it travels: a `harvester is FULL (59x/600)` line rather than `(2/600)`,
and the census dropping by ~600 cells a round (`2400 → 1800 → 1200 …`) instead of
2 at a time. What it does NOT change: the `599/600` sale at the warehouse pad,
the marker-vs-pad refusal, or the reach line that hands the field to the blocks.

**"i think i found a bug" — the logcat had two, and the build's own probes named
both (v1.153).** No symptom was described, so the buffer was read first (rule:
evidence before a fix). Two independent defects were sitting in it, each already
instrumented by an earlier build's honesty line.

**(1) The cultivator has a socket — the line-up just could not read it.** v1.146
added the miss line that lists the hitch/socket-shaped instances a tool *does*
hold, precisely because `has no hitch point (309 instance(s) read)` said nothing
about the game's names for its join (rule 4). Live it answered itself:

```
tool HorschJoker has no hitch point (309 instance(s) read) — what it does hold:
  Objects.Equipment.HorschJoker.Chassis.HitchableSocket [Folder] ·
  Objects.Equipment.HorschJoker.Chassis.HitchableSocket.SocketRoot [Part] ·
  Objects.Equipment.HorschJoker.Chassis.HitchableSocket.SocketRoot.SocketOrigin [Attachment]
  — using creep alignment
… attempts 4…10: hitch prompt never showed within 20 studs — re-aligning
hitch: gave up on HorschJoker after 10 attempt(s) — this pass runs without it
```

**Root cause, from that line and the source, with no guessing:** `hitchPoint`
asked for `Chassis.HitchableSocket` and accepted it **only if it was an
Attachment** — the shape the Tempo planter exposes. On the HorschJoker the game
put the same name on a **Folder** and the point one level deeper, so a tool with
a socket was called socket-less, `findHitchPart` never saw it (the only BasePart
whose name says *hitch* is `Welds.EquipmentRoot->Hitch`, a `Weld`, not a part),
and the creep took all ten attempts at 20 studs each without ever bringing the
prompt up. The cultivate pass then ran unhitched — which is what the census
shows (`998 plowed, 803 cultivated` on a field the run has been cycling for
hours). Global `spudSocketPoint(socket)` now reads every shape the game has
shown — Attachment, Part, or the **shallowest** join inside the folder
(attachment first, part if there is no attachment) — guarded and capped at 64
descendants, returning **nil** rather than inventing a point (rule 7), and the
log names the instance it measured (`HitchableSocket.SocketOrigin (the game's
socket)`). `tools/1000008.py` builds both live trees out of fake
instances and runs the reader on them.

**(2) A 50-seed crate was being taught as 99, and that halved every trip plan.**
v1.145 divided the pour's rise by the crates `spudCratesInHand()` lost; the
buffer shows the divider was as unreliable as the press:

```
✅ second Rice crate bought via FNCRequestStorePurchase (balance 2327524 → 2325524) — the seeder gets both from this one trip
✅ seeder now holds 502/600 seeds (Rice) — the planting pass takes it from here
seeder fill: 502/600 — back to town for more Rice (trip 6 of 8)
seeder fill: the hands are empty and the tank still wants 98 seed(s) of 600 —
             1 more town visit(s) at 2 crate(s) a trip, 99 seed(s) a crate
```

`402/600 → 502/600 → 600/600` is **+100 a trip, two crates** — and the build
still wrote **99 a crate**, so the carry folder answered one box (or zero and the
`or 1` fallback fired). The consequence is arithmetic, not cosmetics: at 99 a
crate, `spudSeedTripsNeeded(803, 0, 99, 2)` is **5**, so the run printed
`the seeder still wants seed and this run already made 6 shop trip(s) (the cap is
5, planned on a remembered tank of 600 for 803 cell(s) at 2 crate(s) a trip)` — a
ceiling *below* the trips already spent, which can only ever refuse — and ended
`run ended — the last stage it reached was: shop trip: the seeder is empty` with
**803 cells cultivated and never planted**. That is the owner's fourth "not
planting the whole field" report, and v1.150's field-sized budget could not help
because the number it divided by was wrong.

**Fix: ask the crate.** Its own `Configurations.Capacity` is the game's number
for exactly this, and `spudCrateSeedCount` has read it since v1.116 to skip an
empty box. Global `spudCrateSize(own, learned)` takes the crate's number when it
can read one — **in either direction**, so it can correct a bad lesson — and the
rise-divided-by-crates survives only as the fallback for a silent box, still only
ever raising (an inference should never shrink a plan). At 50 a crate the same
803 cells plan at **9** visits, which is the truth: 17 crates at two a trip.
`tools/1000033.py` runs the helper and pins the contrast as arithmetic
(9 visits versus the live cap of 5, and each ceiling), and its ties refuse a pour
site that stops asking the crate.

**Noted and deliberately untouched** (rule 16, and rule 4 says it needs a
different piece of evidence): `seed shop: ⚠ the second crate's dialog (Stores) is
still up — close it by hand once` still prints on every two-crate trip even
though the second purchase **lands** (`balance 2327524 → 2325524`) and the pour
succeeds — so it is a noisy verdict about a dialog the till keeps on screen, not
a failed buy, and changing the judge again without a misjudged buy to judge
would be a guess. Same buffer, also honest as shipped: `seed crate: E at the
crate to pick it up — the game offers: nothing` followed by
`✅ carrying the seed crate (EVTRequestToCarry)` is the v1.143 fail-open working
(the remote carries what the billboard does not offer), and the harvester's own
line (`fired 600 of 2400 … 599 took`) is v1.152 doing its job.

**The repo moved to an organization (v1.154).** `simba13231/spud-farm` was
**transferred** to `adwadwadadadawdwqeqweq/spud-farm` (the same repo id, so the
history, stars and issues came with it), which is what keeps the build off the
owner's profile Repositories tab while the raw URL still serves anyone — a
private repo cannot do that, because an executor fetches without credentials.
Fourteen hardcoded URLs in `1000051.lua` (SCRIPT_URL, the DOCTOR's
`commits?per_page=6` call, and the reload's commit probe plus its SHA-built raw
URL), `1000046.lua` (its `RAW` fallback), the five standalone probes' header
comments and the README's Quick start now name the org, and the local
`origin` was repointed. Measured on the transferred repo: the OLD raw path still
answers **200** (GitHub keeps it redirecting), so a loadstring someone saved
before the move keeps working, while `api.github.com/repos/simba13231/...`
answers **301** — the build no longer asks it, but a stale executor copy would
be relying on Roblox following a same-domain redirect.

**Every file is numbered now (v1.155).** The 59 tracked files were renamed to
numeric stems with their extensions kept — `1000051.lua` is the build,
`20000001.md` is this document's user-facing sibling, and the README carries **one
more digit than every other file** at the owner's instruction. `.gitignore` and
`.freebuff/project-id` were left alone on purpose: renaming a `.gitignore` stops
git ignoring anything, which is how private dumps and another tool's config become
committable. Extensions stay because the toolchain needs them (`luau-compile` on a
nameless file, Python on `*.py`) and because an extension says nothing about the
project. Consequences, all handled: the loadstring URL changed (`SpudFarm_Full.lua`
**404s** now — a transferred path redirects, a deleted one does not), so the build
was repointed at itself in `SCRIPT_URL`, in the loader's fallback, in the reload's
SHA-built URL and in every probe header comment; all 51 files that referenced
another by name were rewritten; and `tools/1000032.py` can no longer discover
harnesses by a `*_test.py` glob, so it now excludes the three gates and three
benches by number **and refuses to run green unless it finds exactly 35 test
files** — the failure mode of a rename like this is a gate that silently runs
nothing and prints 4/4. Verified after the change: compile OK, 38/38, headroom 5,
name audit clean 395, and the discovery counts 35 with no typo in either
exclusion list.

**The repo was renamed too (v1.156).** `spud-farm` became
`sdfkjsdflksdakjdsffkjaadkljfdaslk` under the same organization, so the 14 live
URL references (SCRIPT_URL, the loader's two fallbacks, the reload's commit probe
plus its SHA-built raw URL, four README quick-start lines and the three probe
headers) name the new path, and the local `origin` moved with them. Measured on
both names: the old repo path still **serves** the renamed file (raw redirects for
a moved repo), the old *filename* is **404** under both, and
`api.github.com/repos/.../spud-farm/commits` answers **301** where the new name
answers **200** — so the build no longer depends on Roblox following a redirect to
find out which build is newest. The v1.154 entry above keeps `spud-farm` in it
because that is what the transfer targeted at the time.

**"when it does the cultivating or the thing after the plowing it is often missing
1-2 peices of land" — the grid spent calls on cells the band cannot reach
(v1.157).** No crash in the buffer, and one line that says the field is not
finishing without saying why:

```
remote cultivate: fired 1200 of 1200 cell(s) from 140 stop(s), 3x3 per stop — 935 took, 265 left
```

**Root cause, from the build's own arithmetic rather than a new probe:** 140 stops
× 9 = 1260, so the blocks are full squares and v1.140's distance grouping is not
the problem — 22% of the calls were refused, and the refusals are the *far cells*
of each block. `spudBlockSide` sizes the square so that a **centred** blade reaches
its corners (√2 × 2 × 5 = 14.1 studs, inside the 20 that took). But the burst fires
all nine cells from wherever the blade actually sits, and the two tests that
decide whether to fire are looser than the band: the arrival poll accepts
`4 * side` = **12** studs and the miss gate only refuses a stop past **20**. A
blade 10 off its stop centre therefore paints three cells at 24 studs — refused,
unpriced, and *not named anywhere*, because the miss line only fires past 20. The
same stop misses the same way every round, so the residue is always the same 1–2
tiles, which is exactly what the owner sees on the field.

**Fix: don't call what the band cannot take.** `burst(stop, bp)` measures each
cell's **flat** distance from the blade's own position (height is free — 20 was
taken at +8 and +80 straight above was taken, and the corner pass already filters
sideways-only) and skips anything past `spudFieldTaken`, leaving it un-called and
un-`done` so it stays on the field's list for the hop pass, which puts the blade on
each cell. A refused call and a skipped call cost the same field outcome — nothing —
but the skipped one is named: `— 4 cell(s) were past the 20-stud band from where
the blade actually sat, left for the hop`. An unreadable blade position fires the
block whole (rule 7).

`tools/1000016.py` gained a fixture for the residual case: `TRAIL_BIAS`, a tow that
arrives *past* where it was aimed, so the blade lands inside the arrival tolerance
and the old code believed it. The control (a blade that lands on its stop) still
calls and takes all 15 cells; the biased one used to fire 15 and take 10, and now
fires 11 and takes 11. Writing it exposed a model bug in the harness itself: its
fake server measured the band as a **sphere**, so the grid's own +10 hover ate 10
studs of a 20-stud band — which is why one section's `acceptR = 5` had silently
meant "reaches nothing". The band is now flat, matching the measured game, and that
fixture says `acceptR = -1` (the harness's explicit "takes nothing") instead of
inheriting the premise from a height term.
